# Benjamin Netanyahu

Benjamin Netanyahu

Prime Minister of Israel (1996–1999; 2009–2021; 2022–present)

"Netanyahu" redirects here. For his family, see [Netanyahu family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netanyahu_family "Netanyahu family").

**Benjamin** " **Bibi**" **Netanyahu**[&#8202;](#ref-back-4-0)[\[b\]](#refnote-4) (born 21 October 1949) is an Israeli politician and diplomat who has served as [prime minister of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_minister_of_Israel "Prime minister of Israel") since 2022. Having previously held office from 1996 to 1999 and from 2009 to 2021, Netanyahu is [Israel's longest-serving prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_prime_ministers_of_Israel#Tenure_of_office_in_years "List of prime ministers of Israel").[&#8202;](#ref-back-5-0)[\[3\]](#refnote-5) After losing the [2026 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Israeli_legislative_election "2026 Israeli legislative election"), he is set to be dismissed as prime minister on 8 November 2026 and replaced by [Gadi Eisenkot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadi_Eisenkot "Gadi Eisenkot").[&#8202;](#ref-back-6-0)[\[4\]](#refnote-6)

Born in [Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv "Tel Aviv"), Netanyahu was raised in [West Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Jerusalem "West Jerusalem") and the United States. He returned to Israel in 1967 to join the [Israel Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Defense_Forces "Israel Defense Forces") and served in the [Sayeret Matkal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayeret_Matkal "Sayeret Matkal") special forces. In 1972, he returned to the US, and after graduating from the [Massachusetts Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Institute_of_Technology "Massachusetts Institute of Technology"), Netanyahu worked for the [Boston Consulting Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Consulting_Group "Boston Consulting Group"). He moved back to Israel in 1978 and founded [The Jonathan Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonatan_Netanyahu#The_Jonathan_Institute "Yonatan Netanyahu"). Between 1984 and 1988, Netanyahu was [Israel's ambassador to the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel's_ambassador_to_the_United_Nations "Israel's ambassador to the United Nations"). Netanyahu rose to prominence after his [election as chair of Likud in 1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Likud_leadership_election "1993 Likud leadership election"), becoming [leader of the opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leader_of_the_Opposition_(Israel) "Leader of the Opposition (Israel)"). In the [1996 general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Israeli_general_election "1996 Israeli general election"), Netanyahu became the first Israeli prime minister elected directly by popular vote. Netanyahu was defeated in the [1999 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Israeli_general_election "1999 Israeli general election") and entered the private sector. He served as [minister of foreign affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Israel) "Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Israel)") and [finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Finance_(Israel) "Ministry of Finance (Israel)"), initiating [economic reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_economy "Israeli economy"), before resigning over the [Gaza disengagement plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_disengagement_plan "Gaza disengagement plan").

Netanyahu [returned to lead Likud in 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Likud_leadership_election "2005 Likud leadership election"), leading the opposition between 2006 and 2009. After the [2009 legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Israeli_legislative_election "2009 Israeli legislative election"), Netanyahu [formed a coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-second_government_of_Israel "Thirty-second government of Israel") and became prime minister again. Netanyahu made his closeness to [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") central to his appeal from 2016. During [Trump's first presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump's_first_presidency "Trump's first presidency"), the [US recognized Jerusalem as capital of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_Jerusalem_as_capital_of_Israel "United States recognition of Jerusalem as capital of Israel"), [Israeli sovereignty over the Golan Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_the_Golan_Heights_as_part_of_Israel "United States recognition of the Golan Heights as part of Israel"), and brokered the [Abraham Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Accords "Abraham Accords") between Israel and the [Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world "Arab world"). Netanyahu was criticized for expanding [Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_settlement "Israeli settlement") in the [occupied West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupied_West_Bank "Occupied West Bank"), deemed [illegal under international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law_and_Israeli_settlements "International law and Israeli settlements"). In 2019, Netanyahu was [indicted on charges of breach of trust, bribery and fraud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Benjamin_Netanyahu "Trial of Benjamin Netanyahu"), and relinquished all ministerial posts except prime minister. The [2018–2022 Israeli political crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%932022_Israeli_political_crisis "2018–2022 Israeli political crisis") resulted in a [rotation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotation_government "Rotation government") agreement between Netanyahu and [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz "Benny Gantz"). This collapsed in 2020, leading to a [2021 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Israeli_legislative_election "2021 Israeli legislative election"). In June 2021, Netanyahu was removed as prime minister, before returning after the [2022 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Israeli_legislative_election "2022 Israeli legislative election").

Netanyahu's governments have been criticized for perceived [democratic backsliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding "Democratic backsliding") and an alleged shift toward authoritarianism. [His coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-seventh_government_of_Israel "Thirty-seventh government of Israel")'s [2023 judicial reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform "2023 Israeli judicial reform") proposals sparked [mass protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform_protests "2023 Israeli judicial reform protests"), while the [October 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_7_attacks "October 7 attacks") [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas")-led attacks and the ensuing [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war") led to [nationwide protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel#%22Bring_Them_Home_Now%22_protests "Gaza war protests in Israel") over the security lapses and the failure to secure the Israeli hostages' return. His later tenure was marked by escalating regional conflicts and a personal security threat. In 2024, Israel [exchanged strikes with Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Iran%E2%80%93Israel_conflict "2024 Iran–Israel conflict"), while Netanyahu ordered invasions of [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon "2024 Israeli invasion of Lebanon") and [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Syria "2024 Israeli invasion of Syria") and survived [an assassination attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_drone_attack_on_Benjamin_Netanyahu's_residence "2024 drone attack on Benjamin Netanyahu's residence") in October. In 2025, he presided over Israeli strikes on Iran that began the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War "Twelve-Day War"), and in 2026 he launched the [Iran war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_war "2026 Iran war") alongside the United States with the stated goal of [regime change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regime_change_efforts_in_the_2026_Iran_war "Regime change efforts in the 2026 Iran war"), followed by renewed fighting with [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah") that led to [a second invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Lebanon_war "2026 Lebanon war").

Netanyahu's government has been orchestrating the [Gaza genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide "Gaza genocide"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-7-0)[\[5\]](#refnote-7) culminating in the [_South Africa v. Israel_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa's_genocide_case_against_Israel "South Africa's genocide case against Israel") case before the [International Court of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Court_of_Justice "International Court of Justice") (ICJ) in December 2023. The [International Criminal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court "International Criminal Court") (ICC) issued [an arrest warrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICC_arrest_warrants_for_Israeli_leaders "ICC arrest warrants for Israeli leaders") in November 2024 for Netanyahu for alleged [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crime "War crime") and [crimes against humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimes_against_humanity "Crimes against humanity") as part of the [ICC investigation in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Palestine#Arrest_warrants "International Criminal Court investigation in Palestine"). Netanyahu's leadership has resulted in a [substantial decline](#Public_opinion) in [global opinion of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Israel#International_opinion "Foreign relations of Israel").

## Early life, education, and military career

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Netanyahu%27s_military_service_I.jpg/500px-Netanyahu%27s_military_service_I.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Netanyahu's_military_service_I.jpg) 1967 photograph of Netanyahu by the Israel Defense Forces

Netanyahu was born in 1949 in [Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv "Tel Aviv").[&#8202;](#ref-back-8-0)[\[6\]](#refnote-8)[&#8202;](#ref-back-defiant-0)[\[7\]](#refnote-defiant) His mother, Tzila Segal, was born in [Petah Tikva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petah_Tikva "Petah Tikva") in the [Mutasarrifate of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutasarrifate_of_Jerusalem "Mutasarrifate of Jerusalem")—her family had migrated from [Minneapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minneapolis "Minneapolis") in 1911, having relocated there from [Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania "Lithuania") in the 1870s[&#8202;](#ref-back-10-0)[\[8\]](#refnote-10)—and studied law at [Gray's Inn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray's_Inn "Gray's Inn"), London.[&#8202;](#ref-back-11-0)[\[9\]](#refnote-11)[&#8202;](#ref-back-12-0)[\[10\]](#refnote-12)[&#8202;](#ref-back-13-0)[\[11\]](#refnote-13) His father, [Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw "Warsaw")-born [Benzion Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benzion_Netanyahu "Benzion Netanyahu") ( [né](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%A9 "Né") Mileikowsky), was a historian specializing in the [Jewish Golden Age of Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_age_of_Jewish_culture_in_Spain "Golden age of Jewish culture in Spain"). His paternal grandfather, [Nathan Mileikowsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathan_Mileikowsky "Nathan Mileikowsky"), was a [rabbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabbi "Rabbi") and [Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism "Zionism") writer. When Netanyahu's father immigrated to [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine "Mandatory Palestine"), he [adopted the Hebrew surname](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebraization_of_surnames "Hebraization of surnames") "Netanyahu", meaning "God has given". His family is predominantly [Ashkenazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_Jews "Ashkenazi Jews"), but he has said that a DNA test revealed some [Sephardic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sephardi_Jews "Sephardi Jews") ancestry. He claims descent from the [Vilna Gaon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilna_Gaon "Vilna Gaon").[&#8202;](#ref-back-14-0)[\[12\]](#refnote-14)[&#8202;](#ref-back-15-0)[\[13\]](#refnote-15)

Netanyahu was the second of three children. He was initially raised in [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem "Jerusalem"), where he attended Henrietta Szold Elementary School. His evaluation by his 6th-grade teacher Ruth Rubenstein says that Netanyahu was courteous, polite, and helpful; "responsible and punctual"; and friendly, disciplined, cheerful, brave, active, and obedient.[&#8202;](#ref-back-JPost_responsible-0)[\[14\]](#refnote-JPost_responsible)

Between 1956 and 1958, and from 1963 to 1967,[&#8202;](#ref-back-ynetnews.com-0)[\[15\]](#refnote-ynetnews.com) his family lived in the United States in [Cheltenham Township, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheltenham_Township,_Pennsylvania "Cheltenham Township, Pennsylvania"), while Benzion Netanyahu taught at [Dropsie College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dropsie_College "Dropsie College").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Moyer-0)[\[16\]](#refnote-Moyer) Benjamin graduated from [Cheltenham High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheltenham_High_School "Cheltenham High School") and was active in the [debate club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debate "Debate"), [chess club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chess_club "Chess club"), and [soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_school_soccer "High school soccer").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Moyer-1)[\[16\]](#refnote-Moyer) He and his brother Yonatan grew dissatisfied with what they saw as the superficial way of life in the area, including the prevalent youth [counterculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterculture "Counterculture") movement and the liberal sensibilities of the [Reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Judaism "Reform Judaism") synagogue, [Temple Judea of Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_Judea_of_Philadelphia "Temple Judea of Philadelphia"), that the family attended.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Moyer-2)[\[16\]](#refnote-Moyer)

After graduating from high school in 1967, Netanyahu returned to Israel to enlist in the [Israel Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Defense_Forces "Israel Defense Forces"). He trained as a combat soldier and served for five years in a special forces unit of the IDF, [Sayeret Matkal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayeret_Matkal "Sayeret Matkal"). He took part in numerous cross-border raids during the 1967–70 [War of Attrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_Attrition "War of Attrition"), including the March 1968 [Battle of Karameh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Karameh "Battle of Karameh"), when the IDF attacked [Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan "Jordan") to capture [PLO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PLO "PLO") leader [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat "Yasser Arafat") but were repulsed with heavy casualties.[&#8202;](#ref-back-19-0)[\[17\]](#refnote-19)[&#8202;](#ref-back-20-0)[\[18\]](#refnote-20) He became a team-leader in the unit. He was wounded in combat on multiple occasions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ginsburg1-0)[\[19\]](#refnote-ginsburg1) He was involved in many other missions, including the [1968 Israeli raid on Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Israeli_raid_on_Lebanon "1968 Israeli raid on Lebanon") and the rescue of the hijacked [Sabena Flight 571](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabena_Flight_571 "Sabena Flight 571") in May 1972, during which he was shot in the shoulder.[&#8202;](#ref-back-22-0)[\[20\]](#refnote-22)[&#8202;](#ref-back-defiant-1)[\[7\]](#refnote-defiant) He was discharged from active service in 1972 but remained in the Sayeret Matkal reserves. Following his discharge, he left to study in the United States but returned in October 1973 to serve in the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War "Yom Kippur War").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sayeret_Matkal_is_50_years_old-0)[\[21\]](#refnote-Sayeret_Matkal_is_50_years_old)[&#8202;](#ref-back-ball-0)[\[22\]](#refnote-ball)

### Higher education

Netanyahu returned to the United States in late 1972 to study architecture at the [Massachusetts Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Institute_of_Technology "Massachusetts Institute of Technology") (MIT). After returning to Israel to fight in the Yom Kippur War, he returned to the United States and, under the name **Ben Nitay**, completed a [Bachelor of Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor_of_Science "Bachelor of Science")[&#8202;](#ref-back-MITDegrees-0)[\[23\]](#refnote-MITDegrees) in architecture[&#8202;](#ref-back-26-0)[\[24\]](#refnote-26) in February 1975. In 1976 he earned a [master's degree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master's_degree "Master's degree")[&#8202;](#ref-back-MITDegrees-1)[\[23\]](#refnote-MITDegrees) from the [MIT Sloan School of Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIT_Sloan_School_of_Management "MIT Sloan School of Management").[&#8202;](#ref-back-27-0)[\[25\]](#refnote-27)[&#8202;](#ref-back-28-0)[\[26\]](#refnote-28) Concurrently, he was studying toward a doctorate[&#8202;](#ref-back-ball-1)[\[22\]](#refnote-ball) in political science.[&#8202;](#ref-back-29-0)[\[27\]](#refnote-29)[&#8202;](#ref-back-30-0)[\[28\]](#refnote-30) His studies were broken off by the death of his brother, [Yonatan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonatan_Netanyahu "Yonatan Netanyahu"), who led the [Entebbe raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entebbe_raid "Entebbe raid").[&#8202;](#ref-back-ball-2)[\[22\]](#refnote-ball)[&#8202;](#ref-back-31-0)[\[29\]](#refnote-31)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_P.M._Netanyahu_at_Memorial_Service_for_Yoni.jpg/500px-Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_P.M._Netanyahu_at_Memorial_Service_for_Yoni.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_(GPO)_-_P.M._Netanyahu_at_Memorial_Service_for_Yoni.jpg) Benjamin Netanyahu at the grave of his brother [Yoni Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoni_Netanyahu "Yoni Netanyahu"), who was killed leading the [Entebbe raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entebbe_raid "Entebbe raid") in 1976

At MIT, Netanyahu studied a double-load while taking courses at [Harvard University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_University "Harvard University"), completing his bachelor's degree in architecture in two and a half years, despite taking a break to fight in the Yom Kippur War. MIT professor Leon B. Groisser recalled: "He did superbly. He was very bright. Organized. Strong. Powerful. He knew what he wanted to do and how to get it done."[&#8202;](#ref-back-ball-3)[\[22\]](#refnote-ball)

At that time he changed his name to **Benjamin** " **Ben**" **Nitai** (Nitai, a reference to both [Mount Nitai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Nitai "Mount Nitai") and the eponymous Jewish sage [Nittai of Arbela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nittai_of_Arbela "Nittai of Arbela"), a pen name his father often used).[&#8202;](#ref-back-Gresh-0)[\[30\]](#refnote-Gresh) Years later, in an interview with the media, Netanyahu clarified that he decided to do so to make it easier for Americans to pronounce his name. This fact has been used by his political rivals to accuse him indirectly of a lack of Israeli national identity and loyalty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-33-0)[\[31\]](#refnote-33)

## Early career

Netanyahu worked as an economic consultant[&#8202;](#ref-back-34-0)[\[32\]](#refnote-34) for the [Boston Consulting Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Consulting_Group "Boston Consulting Group") in [Boston, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston "Boston"), working at the company between 1976 and 1978. At the Boston Consulting Group, he was a colleague of [Mitt Romney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney "Mitt Romney"), with whom he formed a lasting friendship. Romney described Netanyahu at the time as "a strong personality with a distinct point of view".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Barbaro-0)[\[33\]](#refnote-Barbaro) Netanyahu said that their "easy communication" was a result of "B.C.G.'s intellectually rigorous boot camp".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Barbaro-1)[\[33\]](#refnote-Barbaro)

In 1977, Netanyahu told a dinner party attended by British journalist and historian [Max Hastings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Hastings "Max Hastings") (who was interviewing the family for a book on Yonatan) that in a future war Israel would have the chance to "get all the Arabs out" of the West Bank and Jerusalem.[&#8202;](#ref-back-36-0)[\[34\]](#refnote-36)

In 1978, Netanyahu appeared on Boston local television, under the name Ben Nitay, where he argued: "The real core of the conflict is the unfortunate Arab refusal to accept the State of Israel... For 20 years the Arabs had both the West Bank and the [Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip "Gaza Strip"), and if self-determination, as they now say, is the core of the conflict, they could have easily established a Palestinian state."[&#8202;](#ref-back-37-0)[\[35\]](#refnote-37)[&#8202;](#ref-back-38-0)[\[36\]](#refnote-38)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_Benjamin_Netanyahu_and_Sorin_Hershko.jpg/500px-Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_Benjamin_Netanyahu_and_Sorin_Hershko.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_(GPO)_-_Benjamin_Netanyahu_and_Sorin_Hershko.jpg) Netanyahu (right) with Sorin Hershko, a soldier wounded and permanently paralyzed in [Operation Entebbe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Entebbe "Operation Entebbe"), 2 July 1986

In 1978, Netanyahu returned to Israel. Between 1978 and 1980, he ran the Jonathan Netanyahu Anti-Terror Institute,[&#8202;](#ref-back-ynetnews.com-1)[\[15\]](#refnote-ynetnews.com) a non-governmental organization devoted to the study of terrorism. From 1980 to 1982, he was director of marketing for Rim Industries in Jerusalem.[&#8202;](#ref-back-39-0)[\[37\]](#refnote-39)

[Moshe Arens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Arens "Moshe Arens") appointed him as his Deputy Chief of Mission at the Israeli Embassy in Washington, D.C., while Arens was ambassador to the United States, a position he held from 1982 until 1984.[&#8202;](#ref-back-netanyahu-0)[\[38\]](#refnote-netanyahu) During the [1982 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War "1982 Lebanon War"), he was called up for reserve duty in Sayeret Matkal and requested to be released from service, preferring to remain in the US and serve as a spokesperson for Israel in the wake of harsh international criticism of the war. He presented Israel's case to the media during the war and established a highly efficient public relations system in the Israeli embassy.[&#8202;](#ref-back-41-0)[\[39\]](#refnote-41) Between 1984 and 1988, Netanyahu served as the [Israeli ambassador to the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_ambassador_to_the_United_Nations "Israeli ambassador to the United Nations").[&#8202;](#ref-back-netanyahu-1)[\[38\]](#refnote-netanyahu) Netanyahu was influenced by Rabbi [Menachem M. Schneerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_M._Schneerson "Menachem M. Schneerson"), with whom he formed a relationship during the 1980s. He referred to Schneerson as "the most influential man of our time".[&#8202;](#ref-back-42-0)[\[40\]](#refnote-42)[&#8202;](#ref-back-43-0)[\[41\]](#refnote-43)[&#8202;](#ref-back-44-0)[\[42\]](#refnote-44) Also during the 1980s, Netanyahu became friends with [Fred Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Trump "Fred Trump"), the father of future U.S. president [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump").[&#8202;](#ref-back-nymag.com-0)[\[43\]](#refnote-nymag.com)

## Political career

### Leader of the Opposition (1993–1996)

Prior to the [1988 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Israeli_legislative_election "1988 Israeli legislative election"), Netanyahu returned to Israel and joined the [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud "Likud") party. In the Likud's internal elections, Netanyahu was placed fifth on the party list. Later on he was elected as a Knesset member of the 12th Knesset, and was appointed as a deputy of the foreign minister [Moshe Arens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Arens "Moshe Arens"), and later on [David Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Levy_(Israeli_politician) "David Levy (Israeli politician)"). Netanyahu and Levy did not cooperate and the rivalry between the two only intensified afterwards. During the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War "Gulf War") in early 1991, the English-fluent Netanyahu emerged as the principal spokesman for Israel in media interviews on [CNN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN "CNN") and other news outlets. During the [Madrid Conference of 1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid_Conference_of_1991 "Madrid Conference of 1991") Netanyahu was a member of the Israeli delegation headed by Prime Minister [Yitzhak Shamir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Shamir "Yitzhak Shamir"). After the Madrid Conference Netanyahu was appointed as Deputy Minister in the Israeli Prime Minister's Office.[&#8202;](#ref-back-netanyahu-2)[\[38\]](#refnote-netanyahu)

Following the defeat of the Likud party in the [1992 Israeli legislative elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Israeli_legislative_election "1992 Israeli legislative election") the Likud party [held a party leadership election in 1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Likud_leadership_election "1993 Likud leadership election"), and Netanyahu was victorious, defeating [Benny Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Begin "Benny Begin"), son of the late prime minister [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin "Menachem Begin"), and veteran politician David Levy[&#8202;](#ref-back-46-0)[\[44\]](#refnote-46) (Sharon initially sought Likud party leadership as well, but quickly withdrew when it was evident that he was attracting minimal support). Shamir retired from politics shortly after the Likud's defeat in the 1992 elections.[&#8202;](#ref-back-47-0)[\[45\]](#refnote-47)

Following the [assassination of Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Yitzhak_Rabin "Assassination of Yitzhak Rabin") at the end of a rally in support of the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords "Oslo Accords"), [Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin "Yitzhak Rabin")'s temporary successor [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres "Shimon Peres") decided to call early elections in order to give the government a mandate to advance the peace process.[&#8202;](#ref-back-48-0)[\[46\]](#refnote-48) Netanyahu was the Likud's candidate for prime minister in the [1996 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Israeli_prime_ministerial_election "1996 Israeli prime ministerial election") which took place on 29 May 1996 and were the first Israeli elections in which [Israelis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israelis "Israelis") elected their prime minister directly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Schmemann-0)[\[47\]](#refnote-Schmemann) Netanyahu hired American political operative [Arthur Finkelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_J._Finkelstein "Arthur J. Finkelstein") to run his campaign.[&#8202;](#ref-back-50-0)[\[48\]](#refnote-50) Netanyahu won the 1996 election, becoming the youngest person in the history of the position and the first Israeli prime minister to be born in the State of Israel (Yitzhak Rabin was born in Jerusalem, under the British Mandate of Palestine, prior to the 1948 founding of the Israeli state).[&#8202;](#ref-back-Schmemann-1)[\[47\]](#refnote-Schmemann)

Netanyahu's victory over the pre-election favorite [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres "Shimon Peres") surprised many. The main catalyst in the downfall of the latter was a wave of [suicide bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_suicide_attacks "List of Palestinian suicide attacks") shortly before the elections; on [3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffa_Road_bus_bombings#Second_bombing "Jaffa Road bus bombings") and [4 March 1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dizengoff_Center_suicide_bombing "Dizengoff Center suicide bombing"), Palestinians carried out two [suicide bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_bombing "Suicide bombing"), killing 32 Israelis, with Peres seemingly unable to stop the attacks. During the campaign, Netanyahu stressed that progress in the peace process would be based on the [Palestinian National Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_National_Authority "Palestinian National Authority") fulfilling its obligations – mainly fighting terrorism – and the Likud campaign slogan was, "Netanyahu – making a safe peace". Although Netanyahu won the election for prime minister, Peres's [Israeli Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party "Israeli Labor Party") received more seats in the [Knesset elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Israeli_legislative_election "1996 Israeli legislative election"). Netanyahu had to rely on a coalition with the [ultra-Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haredi_Judaism "Haredi Judaism") parties, [Shas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shas "Shas") and [UTJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Torah_Judaism "United Torah Judaism") in order to form a government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-51-0)[\[49\]](#refnote-51)

### Prime Minister (1996–1999)

Further information: [Twenty-seventh government of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-seventh_government_of_Israel "Twenty-seventh government of Israel")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/P.M._BENJAMIN_NETANYAHU_SHAKING_HANDS_WITH_PALESTINIAN_AUTHORITY_CHAIRMAN_YASSER_ARAFAT_%283x4_cropped%29.jpg/500px-P.M._BENJAMIN_NETANYAHU_SHAKING_HANDS_WITH_PALESTINIAN_AUTHORITY_CHAIRMAN_YASSER_ARAFAT_%283x4_cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:P.M._BENJAMIN_NETANYAHU_SHAKING_HANDS_WITH_PALESTINIAN_AUTHORITY_CHAIRMAN_YASSER_ARAFAT_(3x4_cropped).jpg) Netanyahu's first meeting with Palestinian president [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat "Yasser Arafat") at the [Erez Crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erez_Crossing "Erez Crossing"), 4 September 1996

The months leading up to the 1996 Israeli election were marred by [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas") terrorist attacks in Israel. After the [Shin Bet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shin_Bet "Shin Bet") assassinated Hamas military leader [Yahya Ayyash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahya_Ayyash "Yahya Ayyash") on 5 January 1996, [Mohammed Deif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Deif "Mohammed Deif"), commander of the [Qassam Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qassam_Brigades "Qassam Brigades"), organized a bombing campaign inside Israel as retaliation, including the [Dizengoff Center suicide bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dizengoff_Center_suicide_bombing "Dizengoff Center suicide bombing") and [Jaffa Road bus bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffa_Road_bus_bombings "Jaffa Road bus bombings"). It has been alleged that Syria and Iran had helped in their planning and financing. According to a report, Syrian Defense Minister [Mustafa Tlass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Tlass "Mustafa Tlass") instructed [Ghazi Kanaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghazi_Kanaan "Ghazi Kanaan") to establish links between [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah") and Hamas fighters, who were then trained in [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon") and [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran") and participated in the retaliatory operations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-meib-0)[\[50\]](#refnote-meib)[&#8202;](#ref-back-53-0)[\[51\]](#refnote-53) Hamas operative Hassan Salameh, who planned three of the attacks, was trained in Iran.[&#8202;](#ref-back-54-0)[\[52\]](#refnote-54)

According to [Ronen Bergman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronen_Bergman "Ronen Bergman"), Deif's bombing campaign and the Israeli intelligence services failure to prevent it, was a factor that led to the defeat of Prime Minister [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres "Shimon Peres") and the [Israeli Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party "Israeli Labor Party") in the [1996 Israeli general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Israeli_general_election "1996 Israeli general election") and victory of the [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud "Likud") party of Netanyahu, who opposed the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords "Oslo Accords"). Bergman writes that "after the election, the attacks stopped for almost a year. Some said this was because of [Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat "Yasser Arafat")'s campaign against Hamas, and the arrest of many members of its military wing. Others believed that Hamas no longer had any reason to carry out suicide attacks, because Netanyahu had already almost completely stopped the peace process, which was the short-term goal of the attacks anyway."[&#8202;](#ref-back-55-0)[\[53\]](#refnote-55)

Netanyahu first met Palestinian president Arafat on 4 September 1996. Prior to the meeting, they spoke by telephone.[&#8202;](#ref-back-56-0)[\[54\]](#refnote-56) The meetings continued through Autumn 1996. On their first meeting, Netanyahu said: "I would like to emphasize that we have to take into account the needs and the requirements of both sides on the basis of reciprocity and the assurance of the security and well-being of both Israelis and Palestinian alike." Arafat said: "We are determined to work with Mr. Netanyahu and with his government."[&#8202;](#ref-back-57-0)[\[55\]](#refnote-57) The talks culminated on 14 January 1997, in the signing of the [Hebron Protocol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebron_Protocol "Hebron Protocol").[&#8202;](#ref-back-58-0)[\[56\]](#refnote-58)

In 1996, Netanyahu and [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem "Jerusalem")'s mayor [Ehud Olmert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Olmert "Ehud Olmert") decided to open an exit in the [Muslim Quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Quarter_(Jerusalem) "Muslim Quarter (Jerusalem)") for the [Western Wall Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Wall_Tunnel "Western Wall Tunnel"), which Peres had put on hold for the sake of peace.[&#8202;](#ref-back-59-0)[\[57\]](#refnote-59) This sparked three days of [rioting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Wall_Tunnel#Northern_exit "Western Wall Tunnel") by Palestinians, resulting in dozens of Israelis and Palestinians being killed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-60-0)[\[58\]](#refnote-60)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/President_Clinton_has_lunch_with_King_Hussein_of_Jordan%2C_Prime_Minister_Binyamin_Netanyahu_of_Israel_and_Chairman_Yasser_Arafat_of_the_Palestinian_Authority_in_the_White_House_libra_-_DPLA_-_7704acf9ea5fde994cd2d01f84d89394.jpg/500px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_Clinton_has_lunch_with_King_Hussein_of_Jordan,_Prime_Minister_Binyamin_Netanyahu_of_Israel_and_Chairman_Yasser_Arafat_of_the_Palestinian_Authority_in_the_White_House_libra_-_DPLA_-_7704acf9ea5fde994cd2d01f84d89394.jpg) Netanyahu with President [Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton"), King [Hussein of Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_of_Jordan "Hussein of Jordan") and Palestinian leader [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat "Yasser Arafat") in October 1996

Lack of progress in the peace process led to new negotiations which produced the [Wye River Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wye_River_Memorandum "Wye River Memorandum") in 1998, which detailed the steps to be taken by the Israeli government and [Palestinian Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Authority "Palestinian Authority") to implement the Interim Agreement of 1995. It was signed by Netanyahu and Arafat, and on 17 November 1998, Israel's 120-member parliament, the [Knesset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knesset "Knesset"), approved the Wye River Memorandum by 75–19. Netanyahu publicly emphasized a policy of "three no(s)": no withdrawal from the Golan Heights, no discussion of the status of Jerusalem, and no negotiations under preconditions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-61-0)[\[59\]](#refnote-61)

Despite his public opposition to withdrawing from the [Golan Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights "Golan Heights"), Netanyahu authorized American businessman and former diplomat [Ronald Lauder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Lauder "Ronald Lauder") to conduct secret negotiations with Syrian President [Hafez al-Assad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hafez_al-Assad "Hafez al-Assad") in the late 1990s. In an October 2026 interview with _[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz "Haaretz")_, Lauder said that he had spent approximately 10 to 15 months traveling between Israel, Syria and Lebanon, working with Assad and Lebanese Prime Minister [Rafic Hariri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafic_Hariri "Rafic Hariri") to develop a ten-point framework for a peace agreement. According to Lauder, Assad was prepared to sign the agreement and Netanyahu had accepted its terms, including an Israeli withdrawal from the Golan Heights. Lauder claimed that Netanyahu subsequently refused to sign unless Defense Minister [Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon "Ariel Sharon") endorsed the agreement, effectively ending the negotiations. Netanyahu has previously denied agreeing to relinquish the Golan Heights. Lauder said that Netanyahu had asked him to keep the negotiations confidential and that his refusal to finalize the agreement had permanently damaged their relationship.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Lauder2026-0)[\[60\]](#refnote-Lauder2026)

In 1997 Ali Fallahian, the Iranian Intelligence Minister, authorized a Hamas bombing campaign to disrupt the peace process, and Hamas leader [Khaled Mashal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_Mashal "Khaled Mashal"), picked Mahmoud Abu Hanoud, a bomb-maker in the [West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank "West Bank"), to construct the bombs, and sent [suicide bombers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide_attack "Suicide attack") to detonate them simultaneously in Jerusalem in the 30 July [Mahane Yehuda market bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Mahane_Yehuda_Market_bombings "1997 Mahane Yehuda Market bombings") and 4 September [Ben Yehuda street bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Yehuda_Street_bombings#1997_(5_killed) "Ben Yehuda Street bombings"), killing 21 Israelis.[&#8202;](#ref-back-63-0)[\[61\]](#refnote-63)[&#8202;](#ref-back-64-0)[\[62\]](#refnote-64)[&#8202;](#ref-back-65-0)[\[63\]](#refnote-65) In 1997, Netanyahu authorized a Mossad operation to assassinate Mashal in [Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan "Jordan"), just three years after the two countries had signed a [peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_peace_treaty "Israel–Jordan peace treaty").[&#8202;](#ref-back-inc1-0)[\[64\]](#refnote-inc1) The Mossad team, covering as five Canadian tourists, entered Jordan on 27 September and injected poison into Mashal's ears in a street in [Amman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amman "Amman").[&#8202;](#ref-back-inc1-1)[\[64\]](#refnote-inc1) The plot was exposed and two agents were arrested by Jordanian police while three others hid in the Israeli embassy which was then surrounded by troops.[&#8202;](#ref-back-inc1-2)[\[64\]](#refnote-inc1) An angry [King Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Hussein_of_Jordan "King Hussein of Jordan") demanded Israel to give the antidote and threatened to annul the peace treaty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-inc-0)[\[65\]](#refnote-inc) Netanyahu relented after pressure by US President [Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton") and ordered the release of 61 Jordanian and Palestinian prisoners including Sheikh [Ahmed Yassin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Yassin "Ahmed Yassin").[&#8202;](#ref-back-inc1-3)[\[64\]](#refnote-inc1) The incident sent the nascent Israeli-Jordanian relations plummeting.[&#8202;](#ref-back-inc-1)[\[65\]](#refnote-inc)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Houghton_house_Netanyahu_Albright_Arafat.jpg/500px-Houghton_house_Netanyahu_Albright_Arafat.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Houghton_house_Netanyahu_Albright_Arafat.jpg) Netanyahu sitting with U.S. secretary of state [Madeleine Albright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madeleine_Albright "Madeleine Albright") and Palestinian president [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat "Yasser Arafat") at the [Wye River Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wye_River_Memorandum "Wye River Memorandum"), 1998

Mashal's antidote only secured the release of the two Mossad [Kidon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidon "Kidon") agents carrying out the assassination attempt. At least six other Mossad agents involved were holed up in the Israeli embassy. [King Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_of_Jordan "Hussein of Jordan") would only release them if Israel released [Ahmed Yassin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Yassin "Ahmed Yassin") and a large number of other Palestinian prisoners. Hussein needed the demands to be "enough to enable the king to be able to publicly defend the release of the hit team."[&#8202;](#ref-back-68-0)[\[66\]](#refnote-68)

On the same day that Hamas bombed Ben Yehuda street, [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah") executed the [Ansariya ambush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansariya_ambush "Ansariya ambush") on the IDF's naval special forces [Shayetet 13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shayetet_13 "Shayetet 13"), killing 12 Israeli commandos. On 25 May 1998, the remains and body parts of at least three soldiers who died in the Ansariya ambush were exchanged for 65 Lebanese prisoners and the bodies of 40 Hezbollah fighters and Lebanese soldiers captured by Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-MFA-0)[\[67\]](#refnote-MFA) Netanyahu called it "one of the worst tragedies that has ever occurred to us".[&#8202;](#ref-back-70-0)[\[68\]](#refnote-70)

Netanyahu began a process of economic liberalization, taking steps towards a free-market economy. The government began selling its shares in banks and state-run companies. Netanyahu eased Israel's strict [foreign exchange controls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_exchange_controls "Foreign exchange controls"), enabling Israelis to take an unrestricted amount of money out of the country, open foreign bank accounts, hold foreign currency, and invest freely in other countries.[&#8202;](#ref-back-71-0)[\[69\]](#refnote-71)[&#8202;](#ref-back-72-0)[\[70\]](#refnote-72)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_P.M._Netanyahu_Visiting_the_Western_Wall.jpg/500px-Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_P.M._Netanyahu_Visiting_the_Western_Wall.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_(GPO)_-_P.M._Netanyahu_Visiting_the_Western_Wall.jpg) Netanyahu with his son Yair at the Western Wall in 1998

Netanyahu was opposed by the left and lost support from the right because of his concessions to the Palestinians in [Hebron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebron "Hebron") and elsewhere, and his negotiations with Arafat. Netanyahu lost favor with the public after a chain of scandals involving his marriage and corruption charges. In 1997, [police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Police "Israel Police") recommended Netanyahu be indicted on corruption charges for influence-peddling. He was accused of appointing an attorney general who would reduce the charges but prosecutors ruled there was insufficient evidence to go to trial.[&#8202;](#ref-back-latimes_corruption-0)[\[71\]](#refnote-latimes_corruption) In 1999, Netanyahu faced another scandal when the Israel Police recommended he be tried for corruption for $100,000 in free services from a government contractor; Israel's attorney general did not prosecute, citing difficulties with evidence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-corruption-0)[\[72\]](#refnote-corruption) The Israeli failures against Hamas and Hezbollah under Netanyahu's first premiership, and the resulting release of imprisoned Palestinian and Lebanese leaders dealt a blow to Netanyahu's rhetoric of a "tough stance" towards enemies of Israel, and played a role in his defeat in the [1999 Israeli general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Israeli_general_election "1999 Israeli general election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-75-0)[\[73\]](#refnote-75)

### Political hiatus (1999–2003)

After being defeated by [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak "Ehud Barak") in the [1999 Israeli prime ministerial election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Israeli_prime_ministerial_election "1999 Israeli prime ministerial election"), Netanyahu temporarily retired from politics.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bio-0)[\[74\]](#refnote-bio) He subsequently served as a senior consultant with Israeli communications equipment manufacturer [BATM Advanced Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BATM_Advanced_Communications "BATM Advanced Communications") for two years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-77-0)[\[75\]](#refnote-77)[&#8202;](#ref-back-78-0)[\[76\]](#refnote-78)

With the fall of the Barak government in late 2000, Netanyahu expressed his desire to return to politics. By law, Barak's resignation was supposed to lead to elections for the prime minister position only. Netanyahu insisted that general elections should be held, claiming that otherwise it would be impossible to have a stable government. Netanyahu decided eventually not to run for the prime minister position, a move which facilitated the rise to power of [Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon "Ariel Sharon"), who at the time was considered less popular than Netanyahu. In 2002, after the [Israeli Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party "Israeli Labor Party") left the coalition and vacated the position of foreign minister, Prime Minister Ariel Sharon appointed Netanyahu as foreign minister.[&#8202;](#ref-back-netanyahu-3)[\[38\]](#refnote-netanyahu) Netanyahu challenged Sharon for the leadership of the Likud party in the [2002 Likud leadership election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Likud_leadership_election "2002 Likud leadership election"), but failed to oust him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-79-0)[\[77\]](#refnote-79)

On 9 September 2002, a scheduled speech by Netanyahu at [Concordia University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concordia_University_(Quebec) "Concordia University (Quebec)") in [Montreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreal "Montreal"), Quebec, Canada [was canceled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Netanyahu_riot_at_Concordia_University "Anti-Netanyahu riot at Concordia University") after hundreds of pro-Palestinian protesters overwhelmed security and smashed through a window. Netanyahu was not present at the protest, having remained at his hotel throughout the duration. He later accused the activists of supporting terrorism and "mad zealotry".[&#8202;](#ref-back-80-0)[\[78\]](#refnote-80) Weeks later in October around 200 protesters met Netanyahu outside his [Heinz Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinz_Hall "Heinz Hall") appearance in [Pittsburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh "Pittsburgh"). [Pittsburgh Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh_Police "Pittsburgh Police"), Israeli security and a Pittsburgh [SWAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SWAT "SWAT") unit allowed his speeches to continue downtown at the hall and the [Duquesne Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duquesne_Club "Duquesne Club") as well as suburban [Robert Morris University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Morris_University "Robert Morris University").[&#8202;](#ref-back-81-0)[\[79\]](#refnote-81)

On 12 September 2002, Netanyahu lobbied for the [invasion of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_invasion_of_Iraq "2003 invasion of Iraq"), testifying under oath as a private citizen before the [U.S. House of Representatives Government Reform Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Committee_on_Oversight_and_Government_Reform "United States House Committee on Oversight and Government Reform") regarding the [alleged nuclear threat posed by the Iraqi régime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction "Iraq and weapons of mass destruction"): "There is no question whatsoever that [Saddam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein "Saddam Hussein") is seeking and is working and is advancing towards the development of [nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons "Nuclear weapons")…"[&#8202;](#ref-back-82-0)[\[80\]](#refnote-82)[&#8202;](#ref-back-kerry-0)[\[81\]](#refnote-kerry) He also testified, "If you take out Saddam, [Saddam's regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba'athist_Iraq "Ba'athist Iraq"), I guarantee you that it will have [enormous positive reverberations on the region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationale_for_the_Iraq_War "Rationale for the Iraq War")."[&#8202;](#ref-back-kerry-1)[\[81\]](#refnote-kerry)

### Minister of Finance (2003–2005)

After the [2003 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_Israeli_legislative_election "2003 Israeli legislative election"), in what many observers regarded as a surprise move, Sharon offered the [Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Affairs_Minister_of_Israel "Foreign Affairs Minister of Israel") to [Silvan Shalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvan_Shalom "Silvan Shalom") and offered Netanyahu the [Finance Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finance_Minister_of_Israel "Finance Minister of Israel"). Some pundits speculated that Sharon made the move because he deemed Netanyahu a political threat given his demonstrated effectiveness as foreign minister, and that by placing him in the Finance Ministry during a time of economic uncertainty, he could diminish Netanyahu's popularity. Netanyahu accepted the new appointment. Sharon and Netanyahu came to an agreement that Netanyahu would have complete freedom as finance minister and have Sharon back all of his reforms, in exchange for Netanyahu's silence over Sharon's management of Israel's military and foreign affairs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-marketwatch-0)[\[82\]](#refnote-marketwatch)

As finance minister, Netanyahu undertook an economic plan in order to restore Israel's economy from its low point during the [Second Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Intifada "Second Intifada").[&#8202;](#ref-back-85-0)[\[83\]](#refnote-85)[&#8202;](#ref-back-86-0)[\[84\]](#refnote-86) Netanyahu claimed that a bloated public sector and excessive regulations were largely responsible for stifling economic growth. His plan involved a move toward more [liberalized markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_market "Free market"), although it was not without its critics. He instituted a program to end welfare dependency by requiring people to apply for jobs or training, reduced the size of the public sector, froze government spending for three years, and capped the budget deficit at 1%. The taxation system was streamlined and taxes were cut, with the top individual tax rate reduced from 64% to 44% and the corporate tax rate from 36% to 18%. A host of state assets worth billions of dollars were privatized, including banks, oil refineries, the [El Al](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Al "El Al") national airline, and [Zim Integrated Shipping Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zim_Integrated_Shipping_Services "Zim Integrated Shipping Services"). The retirement ages for both men and women were raised, and currency exchange laws were further liberalized. Commercial banks were forced to spin off their long-term savings. In addition, Netanyahu attacked monopolies and cartels to increase competition. As the Israeli economy started booming and unemployment fell significantly, Netanyahu was widely credited by commentators as having performed an 'economic miracle' by the end of his tenure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-marketwatch-1)[\[82\]](#refnote-marketwatch)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Likud_Leaders_2015-0)[\[85\]](#refnote-Likud_Leaders_2015)[&#8202;](#ref-back-88-0)[\[86\]](#refnote-88)

However, opponents in the Labor party (and even a few within his own Likud) viewed Netanyahu's policies as " [Thatcherite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thatcherite "Thatcherite")" attacks on the venerated Israeli social safety net.[&#8202;](#ref-back-89-0)[\[87\]](#refnote-89) Ultimately, unemployment declined while economic growth soared, the debt-to-GDP ratio dropped to one of the lowest in the world, and foreign investment reached record highs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-marketwatch-2)[\[82\]](#refnote-marketwatch)

Netanyahu threatened to resign from office in 2004 unless the [Gaza pullout plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel's_unilateral_disengagement_plan "Israel's unilateral disengagement plan") was put to a referendum. He later modified the ultimatum and voted for the program in the Knesset, indicating immediately thereafter that he would resign unless a referendum was held within 14 days.[&#8202;](#ref-back-90-0)[\[88\]](#refnote-90) He submitted his resignation letter on 7 August 2005, shortly before the Israeli cabinet voted 17 to 5 to approve the initial phase of withdrawal from [Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip "Gaza Strip").[&#8202;](#ref-back-91-0)[\[89\]](#refnote-91)

### Leader of the Opposition (2006–2009)

Following the withdrawal of Sharon from the Likud, Netanyahu was one of several candidates who vied for the Likud leadership.[&#8202;](#ref-back-xinh-0)[\[90\]](#refnote-xinh) His most recent attempt prior to this was in September 2005 when he had tried to hold early primaries for the position of the head of the Likud party, while the party held the office of prime minister – thus effectively pushing Ariel Sharon out of office. The party rejected this initiative. Netanyahu [retook the leadership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Likud_leadership_election "2005 Likud leadership election") on 20 December 2005, with 47% of the primary vote, to 32% for [Silvan Shalom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvan_Shalom "Silvan Shalom") and 15% for [Moshe Feiglin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Feiglin "Moshe Feiglin").[&#8202;](#ref-back-xinh-1)[\[90\]](#refnote-xinh) In the [March 2006 Knesset elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Israeli_legislative_election "2006 Israeli legislative election"), Likud took the third place behind [Kadima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadima "Kadima") and [Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party "Israeli Labor Party") and Netanyahu served as Leader of the Opposition.[&#8202;](#ref-back-poor-0)[\[91\]](#refnote-poor) On 14 August 2007, Netanyahu was [reelected as chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Likud_leadership_election "2007 Likud leadership election") of the Likud and its candidate for the post of prime minister with 73% of the vote, against [far-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right "Far-right") candidate Moshe Feiglin and World Likud chairman [Danny Danon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Danon "Danny Danon").[&#8202;](#ref-back-feig-0)[\[92\]](#refnote-feig) He opposed the [2008 Israel–Hamas ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_ceasefire "2008 Israel–Hamas ceasefire"), like others in the Knesset opposition. Specifically, Netanyahu said: "This is not a relaxation, it's an Israeli agreement to the rearming of Hamas... What are we getting for this?"[&#8202;](#ref-back-wash-0)[\[93\]](#refnote-wash)

Following [Tzipi Livni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzipi_Livni "Tzipi Livni")'s election to head [Kadima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadima "Kadima") and Olmert's resignation from the post of prime minister, Netanyahu declined to join the coalition Livni was trying to form and supported new elections, which were held in February 2009.[&#8202;](#ref-back-96-0)[\[94\]](#refnote-96)[&#8202;](#ref-back-97-0)[\[95\]](#refnote-97) Netanyahu was the Likud's candidate for prime minister in the [2009 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Israeli_legislative_election "2009 Israeli legislative election") which took place on 10 February 2009, as Livni, the previous [designated acting prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acting_Prime_Minister_of_Israel "Acting Prime Minister of Israel") under the Olmert government, had been unable to form a viable governing coalition. Opinion polls showed Likud in the lead, but with as many as a third of Israeli voters undecided.[&#8202;](#ref-back-98-0)[\[96\]](#refnote-98)

In the election itself, Likud won the second highest number of seats, Livni's party having outnumbered the Likud by one seat. A possible explanation for Likud's relatively poor showing is that some Likud supporters defected to [Avigdor Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Lieberman "Avigdor Lieberman")'s [Yisrael Beiteinu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yisrael_Beiteinu "Yisrael Beiteinu") party. Netanyahu, however, claimed victory on the basis that right-wing parties won the majority of the vote, and on 20 February 2009, Netanyahu was designated by Israeli President [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres "Shimon Peres") to succeed Ehud Olmert as prime minister, and began his negotiations to form a coalition government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-99-0)[\[97\]](#refnote-99)

Despite right wing parties winning a majority of 65 seats in the [Knesset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knesset "Knesset"), Netanyahu preferred a broader centrist coalition and turned to his Kadima rivals, chaired by Tzipi Livni, to join his government. This time it was Livni's turn to decline to join, with a difference of opinion on how to pursue the peace process being the stumbling block. Netanyahu did manage to entice a smaller rival, the [Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party "Israeli Labor Party"), chaired by Ehud Barak, to join his government, giving him a certain amount of centrist tone. Netanyahu presented his cabinet for a Knesset "Vote of Confidence" on 31 March 2009. The 32nd Government was approved that day by a majority of 69 lawmakers to 45 (with five abstaining) and the members were sworn in.[&#8202;](#ref-back-swornh-0)[\[98\]](#refnote-swornh)[&#8202;](#ref-back-swornr-0)[\[99\]](#refnote-swornr)

### Prime Minister (2009–2021)

#### Second term

Further information: [Thirty-second government of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-second_government_of_Israel "Thirty-second government of Israel")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Med-Netan.jpeg/500px-Med-Netan.jpeg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Med-Netan.jpeg) Netanyahu in a meeting with President [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev "Dmitry Medvedev") in Russia, 24 March 2011[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Benjamin_Netanyahu_and_Yohanan_Danino_2012.jpg/500px-Benjamin_Netanyahu_and_Yohanan_Danino_2012.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Benjamin_Netanyahu_and_Yohanan_Danino_2012.jpg) Netanyahu with [Yohanan Danino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yohanan_Danino "Yohanan Danino"), appointed Israel's chief of police in 2011

In 2009, US Secretary of State [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton") voiced support for the establishment of a [Palestinian state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Palestine "State of Palestine")– a solution not endorsed by Netanyahu,[&#8202;](#ref-back-102-0)[\[100\]](#refnote-102) with whom she had pledged US cooperation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-103-0)[\[101\]](#refnote-103) Netanyahu said negotiations with the Palestinians would be conditioned on them recognizing Israel as a Jewish state.[&#8202;](#ref-back-104-0)[\[102\]](#refnote-104)\[ _[unreliable source?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources "Wikipedia:Reliable sources")_\]

During [Obama's 2009 Cairo speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_New_Beginning_(speech) "A New Beginning (speech)") Obama stated that the US, "does not accept the legitimacy of continued Israeli settlements."[&#8202;](#ref-back-unarmed-0)[\[103\]](#refnote-unarmed) On 14 June, ten days after Obama's speech, Netanyahu gave a speech in which he endorsed a "Demilitarized Palestinian State".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hatz_14Jun2009-0)[\[104\]](#refnote-Hatz_14Jun2009) Netanyahu stated he would accept a Palestinian state if [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem "Jerusalem") were to remain [the united capital of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Status_of_Jerusalem "Status of Jerusalem"), the Palestinians would have no army, and give up their demand for a [right of return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_right_of_return "Palestinian right of return"). He argued the right for a "natural growth" in the [existing Jewish settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_settlement "Israeli settlement") in the [West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank "West Bank"), while their permanent status was up for negotiation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unarmed-1)[\[103\]](#refnote-unarmed) He endorsed for the first time a Palestinian state alongside Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-lays-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-lays) He stated he would be willing to meet with any "Arab leader" for negotiations without preconditions, mentioning [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria "Syria"), Saudi Arabia, and [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon").[&#8202;](#ref-back-lays-1)[\[105\]](#refnote-lays) The address represented a new position for Netanyahu's government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-huff-0)[\[106\]](#refnote-huff)

Right-wing members of Netanyahu's coalition criticized his remarks for the creation of a Palestinian State, believing all the land should come under Israeli sovereignty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-109-0)[\[107\]](#refnote-109)[&#8202;](#ref-back-110-0)[\[108\]](#refnote-110) Opposition party [Kadima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadima "Kadima") leader [Tzipi Livni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzipi_Livni "Tzipi Livni") opined that Netanyahu did not really believe in the [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution "Two-state solution") and that his speech was a response to international pressure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Livni-0)[\[109\]](#refnote-Livni) Netanyahu's speech provoked mixed reaction internationally.[&#8202;](#ref-back-rfi-0)[\[110\]](#refnote-rfi) The [Palestinian Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Authority "Palestinian Authority") rejected the conditions on a Palestinian State.[&#8202;](#ref-back-racist-0)[\[111\]](#refnote-racist) Palestinian official [Saeb Erekat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saeb_Erekat "Saeb Erekat") said the speech had "closed the door to permanent status negotiations" due to Netanyahu's declarations on Jerusalem, refugees and settlements.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unarmed-2)[\[103\]](#refnote-unarmed) [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas") said it was "racist"[&#8202;](#ref-back-racist-1)[\[111\]](#refnote-racist) and called on Arab nations to "form stronger opposition".[&#8202;](#ref-back-huff-1)[\[106\]](#refnote-huff) The [Arab League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_League "Arab League") dismissed the speech, declaring that "Arabs would not make concessions regarding issues of Jerusalem and refugees".[&#8202;](#ref-back-xin-0)[\[112\]](#refnote-xin) The EU noted "...this is a step in the right direction. The acceptance of a Palestinian state was present there".[&#8202;](#ref-back-eu1-0)[\[113\]](#refnote-eu1) Obama's [press secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_secretary "Press secretary") said the speech was an "important step forward".[&#8202;](#ref-back-xin-1)[\[112\]](#refnote-xin)[&#8202;](#ref-back-116-0)[\[114\]](#refnote-116) Obama said "this solution can and must ensure both Israel's security and the Palestinians' legitimate aspirations for a viable state".[&#8202;](#ref-back-rfi-1)[\[110\]](#refnote-rfi) France praised the speech and called on Israel to cease building settlements in the West Bank.[&#8202;](#ref-back-rfi-2)[\[110\]](#refnote-rfi)[&#8202;](#ref-back-eu1-1)[\[113\]](#refnote-eu1)

Three months after starting his term, Netanyahu remarked that his cabinet had established a working [national unity government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_government "Coalition government"), and broad consensus for a " [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution "Two-state solution")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-117-0)[\[115\]](#refnote-117) A July 2009 survey found most Israelis supported the government, giving Netanyahu an approval rating of 49 percent.[&#8202;](#ref-back-118-0)[\[116\]](#refnote-118) Netanyahu lifted checkpoints in the West Bank to allow free movement and flow of imports; which resulted in an economic boost.[&#8202;](#ref-back-119-0)[\[117\]](#refnote-119)[&#8202;](#ref-back-120-0)[\[118\]](#refnote-120) In 2009, Netanyahu welcomed the [Arab Peace initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Arab_League_summit "2002 Arab League summit") and lauded a call by [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain "Bahrain")'s [Crown Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Prince "Crown Prince") to normalize relations with Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-121-0)[\[119\]](#refnote-121)[&#8202;](#ref-back-122-0)[\[120\]](#refnote-122)

In August 2009 Netanyahu said: "We want...recognition of Israel as the national state of the Jewish people and...a security settlement".[&#8202;](#ref-back-sofer-0)[\[121\]](#refnote-sofer) Palestinian Authority chairman [Mahmoud Abbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud_Abbas "Mahmoud Abbas") declared he would be willing to meet with Netanyahu at the [UN General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_General_Assembly "UN General Assembly"), where Netanyahu had accepted Obama's invitation for a "triple summit".[&#8202;](#ref-back-124-0)[\[122\]](#refnote-124) Netanyahu was reported to be in a pivotal moment, thinking about a compromise over permission on continuing the already approved construction in the West Bank, in exchange for freezing all settlements thereafter, as well as continuing building in [East Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Jerusalem "East Jerusalem"), and stopping demolition of Arab houses there.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Maariv1-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-Maariv1) In September, it was reported Netanyahu was to agree to settlers' political demands to approve more settlement constructions before a temporary settlement freeze agreement took place.[&#8202;](#ref-back-126-0)[\[124\]](#refnote-126) White House spokesman [Robert Gibbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Gibbs "Robert Gibbs") expressed regret over the move.[&#8202;](#ref-back-127-0)[\[125\]](#refnote-127)

On 7 September, Netanyahu left his office without reporting his destination. His military secretary reported Netanyahu had visited a security facility in Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-dis-0)[\[126\]](#refnote-dis) On 9 September, _[Yedioth Ahronoth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yedioth_Ahronoth "Yedioth Ahronoth")_ reported that he had made a secret flight to Moscow to try to persuade Russian officials not to sell S-300 anti-aircraft missile systems to Iran.[&#8202;](#ref-back-dis-1)[\[126\]](#refnote-dis)[&#8202;](#ref-back-129-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-129)[&#8202;](#ref-back-130-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-130) Headlines branded Netanyahu a "liar" and dubbed the affair a "fiasco".[&#8202;](#ref-back-131-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-131)[&#8202;](#ref-back-132-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-132) The military secretary was reportedly dismissed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-133-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-133) _[The Sunday Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sunday_Times "The Sunday Times")_ reported that the trip was made to share the names of Russian scientists Israel believed were abetting Iran's nuclear weapons program.[&#8202;](#ref-back-134-0)[\[132\]](#refnote-134)

On 24 September 2009, in an address to the UN General Assembly, Netanyahu said Iran posed a threat to world peace and it was incumbent on the UN to prevent it from obtaining nuclear weapons.[&#8202;](#ref-back-un-0)[\[133\]](#refnote-un)[&#8202;](#ref-back-un-1)[\[134\]](#refnote-un) Waving the blueprints for Auschwitz and invoking the memory of his family members murdered by the Nazis, Netanyahu delivered a riposte to Iranian president Mahmoud Ahmadinejad's questioning of the Holocaust, asking: "Have you no shame?"[&#8202;](#ref-back-137-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-137)

In response to pressure from the Obama administration urging the resumption of peace talks, on 25 November Netanyahu announced a partial 10-month settlement construction freeze. It had no significant effect on actual settlement construction.[&#8202;](#ref-back-138-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-138) U.S. special envoy [George J. Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_J._Mitchell "George J. Mitchell") said, "while the United States shares Arab concerns about the limitations of Israel's gesture, it is more than any Israeli government has ever done".[&#8202;](#ref-back-139-0)[\[137\]](#refnote-139) Netanyahu called the move "a painful step that will encourage the peace process" and urged the Palestinians to respond.[&#8202;](#ref-back-140-0)[\[138\]](#refnote-140) The Palestinians rejected the call, stating the gesture was "insignificant" in that thousands of approved settlement buildings in the West Bank would continue to be built and there would be no freeze of settlement activity in East Jerusalem.[&#8202;](#ref-back-141-0)[\[139\]](#refnote-141)

In March 2010, Israel's government approved construction of an additional 1,600 apartments in a Jewish housing development in northeast Jerusalem called [Ramat Shlomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramat_Shlomo "Ramat Shlomo")[&#8202;](#ref-back-jp2010-0)[\[140\]](#refnote-jp2010-03) despite the US position that such acts thwart peace talks. Israel's announcement occurred during a visit by U.S. Vice President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") and the US publicly condemned the plan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-t2010-0)[\[141\]](#refnote-t2010-03) Netanyahu issued a statement that all previous Israeli governments had continuously permitted construction in the neighborhood, and certain neighborhoods had always been included as part of Israel in any final agreement plan proposed by either side.[&#8202;](#ref-back-jp2010-1)[\[140\]](#refnote-jp2010-03) Netanyahu regretted the timing of the announcement but asserted that "our policy on Jerusalem is the same policy followed by all Israeli governments for the 42 years...it has not changed."[&#8202;](#ref-back-144-0)[\[142\]](#refnote-144)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Clinton_Netanyahu_Abbas_Mitchell_2_Sep_2010.jpg/500px-Clinton_Netanyahu_Abbas_Mitchell_2_Sep_2010.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Clinton_Netanyahu_Abbas_Mitchell_2_Sep_2010.jpg) Netanyahu, [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton"), [George J. Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_J._Mitchell "George J. Mitchell") and [Mahmoud Abbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahmoud_Abbas "Mahmoud Abbas") at the start of the [direct talks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_negotiations_between_Israel_and_the_Palestinians_(2010-2011) "Direct negotiations between Israel and the Palestinians (2010-2011)"), 2 September 2010

In September 2010, Netanyahu agreed to enter [direct talks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_negotiations_between_Israel_and_the_Palestinians_in_2010 "Direct negotiations between Israel and the Palestinians in 2010"), mediated by the [Obama administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Barack_Obama "Presidency of Barack Obama"), with the Palestinians.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ap090110-0)[\[143\]](#refnote-ap090110) The aim was a "final status settlement" to the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict "Israeli–Palestinian conflict") by forming a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution "Two-state solution") for the Jewish and Palestinian people. On 27 September, the 10-month settlement freeze ended, and the Israeli government approved new construction in the West Bank, including East Jerusalem.[&#8202;](#ref-back-146-0)[\[144\]](#refnote-146) On retirement in 2011, US Defense Secretary [Robert Gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Gates "Robert Gates") said Netanyahu was ungrateful to the US and endangering Israel. The Likud party defended Netanyahu by saying most Israelis supported him and he had broad support in the US.[&#8202;](#ref-back-147-0)[\[145\]](#refnote-147)[&#8202;](#ref-back-148-0)[\[146\]](#refnote-148) In 2012, Netanyahu officially recognized for the first time the right for Palestinians to have their own state in an official document, a letter to Mahmoud Abbas, though as before[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hatz_14Jun2009-1)[\[104\]](#refnote-Hatz_14Jun2009) he declared it would have to be demilitarized.[&#8202;](#ref-back-149-0)[\[147\]](#refnote-149)

Netanyahu unsuccessfully called for the early release of [Jonathan Pollard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Pollard "Jonathan Pollard"), an American serving a life sentence for passing secret US documents to Israel in 1987.[&#8202;](#ref-back-150-0)[\[148\]](#refnote-150) He raised the issue at the [Wye River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wye_River_(Maryland) "Wye River (Maryland)") Summit in 1998, where he claimed president Bill Clinton had privately agreed to release Pollard.[&#8202;](#ref-back-151-0)[\[149\]](#refnote-151)[&#8202;](#ref-back-152-0)[\[150\]](#refnote-152) In 2002, Netanyahu visited Pollard at his [North Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Carolina "North Carolina") prison.[&#8202;](#ref-back-153-0)[\[151\]](#refnote-153)[&#8202;](#ref-back-154-0)[\[152\]](#refnote-154) Netanyahu maintained contact with Pollard's wife, and pressed the Obama administration to release Pollard.[&#8202;](#ref-back-155-0)[\[153\]](#refnote-155)[&#8202;](#ref-back-lettertoobama-0)[\[154\]](#refnote-lettertoobama)

[2011 Israeli social justice protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Israeli_social_justice_protests "2011 Israeli social justice protests") saw hundreds of thousands protest Israel's high cost of living. Netanyahu appointed the [Trajtenberg Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trajtenberg_Committee "Trajtenberg Committee") and it submitted recommendations to lower living costs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-157-0)[\[155\]](#refnote-157) Although Netanyahu promised to push the proposed reforms through cabinet in one piece, differences inside his coalition resulted in gradual adoption.[&#8202;](#ref-back-158-0)[\[156\]](#refnote-158)

Netanyahu's cabinet approved a plan to build a [fiber-optic cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optical_fiber_cable "Optical fiber cable") network to bring cheap, high-speed [fiber-optic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiber-optic_communication "Fiber-optic communication") Internet access to every home.[&#8202;](#ref-back-159-0)[\[157\]](#refnote-159)[&#8202;](#ref-back-160-0)[\[158\]](#refnote-160)

In 2012, Netanyahu planned to call early elections, but oversaw the creation of a government of national unity to see Israel through until the 2013 elections.[&#8202;](#ref-back-161-0)[\[159\]](#refnote-161) In October 2012, Netanyahu and Foreign Minister [Avigdor Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Lieberman "Avigdor Lieberman") announced that their parties, [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud "Likud") and [Yisrael Beiteinu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yisrael_Beiteinu "Yisrael Beiteinu"), had merged and would run together on a single ballot in Israel's 2013 elections.[&#8202;](#ref-back-162-0)[\[160\]](#refnote-162)

#### Third term

Further information: [Thirty-third government of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-third_government_of_Israel "Thirty-third government of Israel")

The [2013 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Israeli_legislative_election "2013 Israeli legislative election") returned Netanyahu's [Likud Beiteinu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud_Yisrael_Beiteinu "Likud Yisrael Beiteinu") coalition with 11 fewer seats than the combined Likud and Yisrael Beiteinu parties had going into the vote.[&#8202;](#ref-back-163-0)[\[161\]](#refnote-163) Israeli president Shimon Peres charged Netanyahu with the task of forming the [Thirty-third government of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-third_government_of_Israel "Thirty-third government of Israel").[&#8202;](#ref-back-164-0)[\[162\]](#refnote-164)[&#8202;](#ref-back-swornh-1)[\[98\]](#refnote-swornh)[&#8202;](#ref-back-swornr-1)[\[99\]](#refnote-swornr) During Netanyahu's third term, he continued his policy of economic liberalization. In December 2013, the Knesset approved the Business Concentration Law, which intended to open Israel's highly concentrated economy to competition to lower consumer prices, reduce income inequality, and increase economic growth. Netanyahu had formed the Concentration Committee in 2010, and the bill, which was pushed forward by his government, implemented its recommendations. The new law banned multi-tiered corporate holding structures, in which a CEO's family members or other affiliated individuals held public companies which in turn owned other public companies, and who were thus able to engage in [price gouging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Price_gouging "Price gouging"). Under the law, corporations were banned from owning more than two tiers of publicly listed companies and from holding both financial and non-financial enterprises.
