All conglomerates were given four to six years to sell excess holdings.[&#8202;](#ref-back-165-0)[\[163\]](#refnote-165)[&#8202;](#ref-back-166-0)[\[164\]](#refnote-166) Netanyahu also began a campaign of port privatization to break what he viewed as the monopoly held by workers of the [Israel Port Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Port_Authority "Israel Port Authority"), so as to lower consumer prices and increase exports. In July 2013, he issued tenders for the construction of private ports in [Haifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haifa "Haifa") and [Ashdod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashdod "Ashdod").[&#8202;](#ref-back-167-0)[\[165\]](#refnote-167) Netanyahu has also pledged to curb excess bureaucracy and regulations to ease the burden on industry.[&#8202;](#ref-back-168-0)[\[166\]](#refnote-168)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Visita_a_Israel_para_el_Funeral_de_Estado_de_Shimon_Peres_%2830022263895%29.jpg/500px-Visita_a_Israel_para_el_Funeral_de_Estado_de_Shimon_Peres_%2830022263895%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Visita_a_Israel_para_el_Funeral_de_Estado_de_Shimon_Peres_(30022263895).jpg) Netanyahu, U.S. president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") and Israeli president [Reuven Rivlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuven_Rivlin "Reuven Rivlin") at the funeral of former Israeli president [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres "Shimon Peres"), Jerusalem, 30 September 2016

In April and June 2014, Netanyahu spoke of his deep concerns when Hamas and the Palestinian Authority agreed and then formed a unity government, and was severely critical of both the United States and European governments' decision to work with the Palestinian coalition government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-169-0)[\[167\]](#refnote-169) He blamed Hamas for [the kidnapping and murder of three Israeli teenagers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_kidnapping_and_murder_of_Israeli_teenagers "2014 kidnapping and murder of Israeli teenagers") in June 2014,[&#8202;](#ref-back-170-0)[\[168\]](#refnote-170) and launched a massive search and arrest operation on the West Bank, targeting members of Hamas in particular, and over the following weeks hit 60 targets in Gaza.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Leas-0)[\[169\]](#refnote-Leas) Missile and rocket exchanges between Gaza militants and the IDF escalated after the bodies of the teenagers, who had been killed almost immediately as the government had good reasons to suspect, were discovered on 30 June 2014.[&#8202;](#ref-back-172-0)[\[170\]](#refnote-172) After several [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas") operatives were killed, Hamas officially declared it would launch rockets from Gaza into Israel,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Leas-1)[\[169\]](#refnote-Leas)[&#8202;](#ref-back-173-0)[\[171\]](#refnote-173) and Israel started [Operation Protective Edge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Protective_Edge "Operation Protective Edge") in the Gaza Strip, formally ending the November 2012 ceasefire agreement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-174-0)[\[172\]](#refnote-174) The prime minister did a round of television shows in the United States and described Hamas as "genocidal terrorists" in an interview on CNN.[&#8202;](#ref-back-IsraelPM-0)[\[173\]](#refnote-IsraelPM) When asked if Gazan casualties from the operation might spark "a third intifada", Netanyahu replied that Hamas was working towards that goal.[&#8202;](#ref-back-176-0)[\[174\]](#refnote-176)

In October 2014, Netanyahu's government approved a privatization plan to reduce corruption and politicization in government companies, and strengthen Israel's capital market. Under the plan, minority stakes of up to 49% in state-owned companies, including arms manufacturers, energy, postal, water, and railway companies, as well as the ports of Haifa and Ashdod.[&#8202;](#ref-back-177-0)[\[175\]](#refnote-177) That same month, Netanyahu called criticism of settlements "against the American values", which earned him rebuke from the White House Press Secretary Josh Earnest, who said that American values had resulted in Israel receiving consistent funding and protective technology such as Iron Dome. Netanyahu explained that he does not accept residency restrictions for Jews, and said that Jerusalem's Arabs and Jews should be able to buy homes wherever they want. He said he was "baffled" by the American condemnation. "It's against the American values. And it doesn't bode well for peace. The idea that we'd have this ethnic purification as a condition for peace, I think it's anti-peace."[&#8202;](#ref-back-178-0)[\[176\]](#refnote-178)[&#8202;](#ref-back-179-0)[\[177\]](#refnote-179)[&#8202;](#ref-back-180-0)[\[178\]](#refnote-180) Later, Jeffrey Goldberg of _[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic "The Atlantic")_ reported that the relationship between Netanyahu and the White House had reached a new low, with the U.S. administration angry over Israel's settlement policies, and Netanyahu expressing contempt for the American administration's grasp of the Middle East.[&#8202;](#ref-back-crisis-0)[\[179\]](#refnote-crisis)

On 2 December 2014, Netanyahu fired ministers [Yair Lapid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yair_Lapid "Yair Lapid"), head of [Yesh Atid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yesh_Atid "Yesh Atid"), and [Tzipi Livni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzipi_Livni "Tzipi Livni"), head of [Hatnua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatnua "Hatnua"). The changes led to the dissolution of the government, with new elections on 17 March 2015.[&#8202;](#ref-back-182-0)[\[180\]](#refnote-182)

[Benjamin Netanyahu's 2015 address to the United States Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu's_2015_address_to_the_United_States_Congress "Benjamin Netanyahu's 2015 address to the United States Congress") marked Netanyahu's third speech to a joint session of Congress.[&#8202;](#ref-back-183-0)[\[181\]](#refnote-183) The day before announcing he would address Congress, _[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_(magazine) "Time (magazine)")_ reported that he tried to derail a meeting between U.S. lawmakers and the head of Mossad, [Tamir Pardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamir_Pardo "Tamir Pardo"), who intended warning them against imposing further sanctions against Iran, a move that might derail nuclear talks.[&#8202;](#ref-back-184-0)[\[182\]](#refnote-184)[&#8202;](#ref-back-185-0)[\[183\]](#refnote-185) Leading up to the speech, Israeli consuls general in the United States "expect\[ed\] fierce negative reaction from U.S. Jewish communities and Israel's allies". Objections included the arrangement of the speech without the support and engagement of the Obama administration and the timing of the speech before Israel's March 2015 election. Seven American Jewish lawmakers met with [Ron Dermer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Dermer "Ron Dermer"), Israel's ambassador to the U.S. and recommended that Netanyahu instead meet with lawmakers privately to discuss Iran.[&#8202;](#ref-back-186-0)[\[184\]](#refnote-186) In making the speech, Netanyahu claimed to speak for all Jews worldwide, a claim disputed by others in the Jewish community.[&#8202;](#ref-back-187-0)[\[185\]](#refnote-187)[&#8202;](#ref-back-188-0)[\[186\]](#refnote-188)[&#8202;](#ref-back-189-0)[\[187\]](#refnote-189)[&#8202;](#ref-back-190-0)[\[188\]](#refnote-190)

As election day approached in what was perceived to be a close race in the 2015 Israeli elections, Netanyahu answered 'indeed' when asked whether a Palestinian state would not be established in his term. He said that support of a Palestinian state is tantamount to yielding territory for radical Islamic terrorists to attack Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-191-0)[\[189\]](#refnote-191) However, Netanyahu reiterated "I don't want a one-state solution. I want a peaceful, sustainable two-state solution. I have not changed my policy."[&#8202;](#ref-back-192-0)[\[190\]](#refnote-192)

#### Fourth term

Further information: [Thirty-fourth government of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-fourth_government_of_Israel "Thirty-fourth government of Israel")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/SecStae_viasit_April_2018_%2841884908901%29.jpg/500px-SecStae_viasit_April_2018_%2841884908901%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:SecStae_viasit_April_2018_(41884908901).jpg) Netanyahu and US secretary of state [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo "Mike Pompeo")[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Joseph_Dunford_visit_to_Israel%2C_May_2017_DSC_3508_%2833798189093%29.jpg/500px-Joseph_Dunford_visit_to_Israel%2C_May_2017_DSC_3508_%2833798189093%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Joseph_Dunford_visit_to_Israel,_May_2017_DSC_3508_(33798189093).jpg) Netanyahu, [Joseph Dunford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Dunford "Joseph Dunford") and Jewish veterans of the [Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Army "Red Army"), [Victory Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Day_(9_May) "Victory Day (9 May)") in Jerusalem, 9 May 2017[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/President_Trump_at_the_Israel_Museum._Jerusalem_May_23%2C_2017_President_Trump_at_the_Israel_Museum._Jerusalem_May_23%2C_2017_%2834460980460%29.jpg/500px-President_Trump_at_the_Israel_Museum._Jerusalem_May_23%2C_2017_President_Trump_at_the_Israel_Museum._Jerusalem_May_23%2C_2017_%2834460980460%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_Trump_at_the_Israel_Museum._Jerusalem_May_23,_2017_President_Trump_at_the_Israel_Museum._Jerusalem_May_23,_2017_(34460980460).jpg) Netanyahu meets with President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") in Jerusalem, May 2017.[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/President_of_Ukraine_held_a_meeting_with_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel%2C_January_2018.III.jpeg/500px-President_of_Ukraine_held_a_meeting_with_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel%2C_January_2018.III.jpeg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_of_Ukraine_held_a_meeting_with_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel,_January_2018.III.jpeg) Netanyahu meets with Ukrainian president [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko "Petro Poroshenko"), 24 January 2018.[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/President_Trump_Welcome_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel_to_the_White_House%2C_25_March_2019.jpg/500px-President_Trump_Welcome_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel_to_the_White_House%2C_25_March_2019.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_Trump_Welcome_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel_to_the_White_House,_25_March_2019.jpg) President Trump, joined by Netanyahu behind, signs the proclamation [recognizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_the_Golan_Heights_as_part_of_Israel "United States recognition of the Golan Heights as part of Israel") Israel's 1981 [annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights_Law "Golan Heights Law") of the [Golan Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights "Golan Heights"), March 2019.

In the [2015 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Israeli_legislative_election "2015 Israeli legislative election"), Netanyahu returned with his party Likud leading the elections with 30 mandates, making it the single highest number of seats for the Knesset. President Rivlin granted Netanyahu an extension until 6 May 2015 to build a coalition when one had not been finalized in the first four weeks of negotiations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-193-0)[\[191\]](#refnote-193) He formed a coalition government within two hours of the midnight 6 May deadline.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TimesIsrael05062015-0)[\[192\]](#refnote-TimesIsrael05062015) His Likud party formed the coalition with [Jewish Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jewish_Home "The Jewish Home"), [United Torah Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Torah_Judaism "United Torah Judaism"), [Kulanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kulanu "Kulanu"), and [Shas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shas "Shas").[&#8202;](#ref-back-TimesIsrael05062015-1)[\[192\]](#refnote-TimesIsrael05062015)[&#8202;](#ref-back-195-0)[\[193\]](#refnote-195)

In August 2015, Netanyahu's government approved a two-year budget that would see agricultural reforms and lowering of import duties to reduce [food prices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_prices "Food prices"), deregulation of the approval process in construction to lower housing costs and speed up infrastructure building, and reforms in the financial sector to boost competition and lower fees for financial services.[&#8202;](#ref-back-196-0)[\[194\]](#refnote-196)[&#8202;](#ref-back-197-0)[\[195\]](#refnote-197) In the end, the government was forced to compromise by removing some key agricultural reforms.[&#8202;](#ref-back-198-0)[\[196\]](#refnote-198)

In October 2015, Netanyahu caused commotion for saying the Grand Mufti of Jerusalem, [Haj Amin al-Husseini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haj_Amin_al-Husseini "Haj Amin al-Husseini") gave [Adolf Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Hitler "Adolf Hitler") the idea of exterminating Jews rather than expelling them during the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II "World War II").[&#8202;](#ref-back-199-0)[\[197\]](#refnote-199)[&#8202;](#ref-back-200-0)[\[198\]](#refnote-200)[&#8202;](#ref-back-201-0)[\[199\]](#refnote-201)[&#8202;](#ref-back-202-0)[\[200\]](#refnote-202) This claim is dismissed by most historians,[&#8202;](#ref-back-203-0)[\[201\]](#refnote-203) who say that al-Husseini's meeting with Hitler took place approximately five months after the mass murder of Jews began.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hatz_months_before-0)[\[202\]](#refnote-Hatz_months_before) Some of the strongest criticism came from Israeli academics: [Yehuda Bauer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehuda_Bauer "Yehuda Bauer") said Netanyahu's claim was "completely idiotic".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Hatz_months_before-1)[\[202\]](#refnote-Hatz_months_before) [Moshe Zimmermann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Zimmermann "Moshe Zimmermann") stated that "any attempt to deflect the burden from Hitler to others is a form of [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial "Holocaust denial")."[&#8202;](#ref-back-205-0)[\[203\]](#refnote-205)

In March 2016, Netanyahu's coalition faced a potential crisis as ultra-Orthodox members threatened to withdraw over the government's proposed steps to create non-Orthodox prayer space at the [Western Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Wall "Western Wall"). They have stated they will leave the coalition if the government offers any further official state recognition of [Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Judaism "Conservative Judaism") and [Reform Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Judaism "Reform Judaism").[&#8202;](#ref-back-206-0)[\[204\]](#refnote-206)

On 23 December 2016, the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States"), under the [Obama Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obama_Administration "Obama Administration"), abstained from [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2334](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2334 "United Nations Security Council Resolution 2334"), effectively allowing it to pass.[&#8202;](#ref-back-207-0)[\[205\]](#refnote-207) On 28 December, U.S. Secretary of State [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry "John Kerry") strongly criticized Israel and its [settlement policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_settlement "Israeli settlement") in a speech.[&#8202;](#ref-back-208-0)[\[206\]](#refnote-208) Netanyahu strongly criticized both the UN Resolution[&#8202;](#ref-back-209-0)[\[207\]](#refnote-209) and Kerry's speech[&#8202;](#ref-back-210-0)[\[208\]](#refnote-210) in response. On 6 January 2017, the Israeli government withdrew its annual dues from the organization, which totaled $6million in [United States dollars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_dollar "United States dollar").[&#8202;](#ref-back-211-0)[\[209\]](#refnote-211)

In February 2017, Netanyahu became the first serving prime minister of Israel to visit [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia "Australia"). He was accompanied by his wife, Sara. The three-day official visit included a delegation of business representatives, and Netanyahu and [Prime Minister of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Australia "Prime Minister of Australia") [Malcolm Turnbull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malcolm_Turnbull "Malcolm Turnbull") were scheduled to sign several bilateral agreements. Netanyahu recalled that it was the [Australian Light Horse regiments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Light_Horse#Formation_and_organisation "Australian Light Horse") that [liberated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Beersheba_(1917) "Battle of Beersheba (1917)") [Beersheba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beersheba "Beersheba") during [World War I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_I "World War I"), and this began what has been a relationship of 100 years between the countries.[&#8202;](#ref-back-2017-02-22_ABC-0)[\[210\]](#refnote-2017-02-22_ABC)

In October 2017, shortly after the US announced the same action, Netanyahu's government announced it was leaving [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") due to what it saw as anti-Israel actions by the agency,[&#8202;](#ref-back-213-0)[\[211\]](#refnote-213)[&#8202;](#ref-back-214-0)[\[212\]](#refnote-214) and it made that decision official in December 2017.[&#8202;](#ref-back-215-0)[\[213\]](#refnote-215)[&#8202;](#ref-back-216-0)[\[214\]](#refnote-216) The Israeli government officially notified UNESCO of the withdrawal in late December 2017.[&#8202;](#ref-back-217-0)[\[215\]](#refnote-217)[&#8202;](#ref-back-218-0)[\[216\]](#refnote-218)[&#8202;](#ref-back-219-0)[\[217\]](#refnote-219)[&#8202;](#ref-back-220-0)[\[218\]](#refnote-220)

In April 2018, Netanyahu accused Iran of not holding up its end of the [Iran nuclear deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action "Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action") after presenting [a cache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Amad "Project Amad") of over 100,000 documents detailing the extent of Iran's nuclear program. Iran denounced Netanyahu's presentation as "propaganda".[&#8202;](#ref-back-221-0)[\[219\]](#refnote-221)

Netanyahu praised the [2018 North Korea–United States Singapore Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_North_Korea%E2%80%93United_States_Singapore_Summit "2018 North Korea–United States Singapore Summit"). He said in a statement, "I commend US President Donald Trump on the historic summit in Singapore. This is an important step in the effort to rid the Korean peninsula of nuclear weapons."[&#8202;](#ref-back-222-0)[\[220\]](#refnote-222)

In July 2018, the Knesset passed the [Nation-State Bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Law:_Israel_as_the_Nation-State_of_the_Jewish_People "Basic Law: Israel as the Nation-State of the Jewish People"), a [Basic Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Laws_of_Israel "Basic Laws of Israel") supported by Netanyahu's coalition government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-223-0)[\[221\]](#refnote-223)[&#8202;](#ref-back-224-0)[\[222\]](#refnote-224)[&#8202;](#ref-back-225-0)[\[223\]](#refnote-225) Analysts saw the bill as a sign of Netanyahu's coalition advancing a right-wing agenda.[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[224\]](#refnote-226)

Prior to the [April 2019 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2019_Israeli_legislative_election "April 2019 Israeli legislative election"), Netanyahu helped broker a deal that united the [Jewish Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jewish_Home "The Jewish Home") party[&#8202;](#ref-back-jpost.com-0)[\[225\]](#refnote-jpost.com) with the far-right [Otzma Yehudit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otzma_Yehudit "Otzma Yehudit") party, in order to form the [Union of the Right-Wing Parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_the_Right-Wing_Parties "Union of the Right-Wing Parties"). The motivation of the deal was to overcome the [electoral threshold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_threshold "Electoral threshold") for smaller parties. The deal was criticized in the media, as Otzma is widely characterized as racist and traces its origins to the extremist [Kahanist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kahanism "Kahanism") movement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[226\]](#refnote-228)[&#8202;](#ref-back-229-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-229)

##### Criminal investigations and indictment

Main article: [Trial of Benjamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Benjamin_Netanyahu "Trial of Benjamin Netanyahu")

Since January 2017, Netanyahu has been investigated by Israeli police in two connected cases, "Case 1000" and "Case 2000". In Case 1000, Netanyahu is suspected of having obtained inappropriate favors from businessmen, including [James Packer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Packer "James Packer") and Hollywood producer [Arnon Milchan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnon_Milchan "Arnon Milchan").[&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-230)[&#8202;](#ref-back-231-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-231) Case 2000 involves alleged attempts to strike a deal with the publisher of the _Yedioth Ahronot_ newspaper group, [Arnon Mozes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnon_Mozes "Arnon Mozes"), to promote legislation to weaken _Yedioth's_ main competitor in exchange for more favorable political coverage.

In August 2017, Israeli police confirmed that Netanyahu was suspected of crimes involving fraud, breach of trust, and bribes in the two cases.[&#8202;](#ref-back-232-0)[\[230\]](#refnote-232) The next day, it was reported that the prime minister's former chief of staff, [Ari Harow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ari_Harow "Ari Harow"), had signed a deal with prosecutors to testify against Netanyahu.[&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-233)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Jerusalem_demonstrations_06.jpg/500px-Jerusalem_demonstrations_06.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jerusalem_demonstrations_06.jpg) [Israelis protest against Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Benjamin_Netanyahu_(2020) "Protests against Benjamin Netanyahu (2020)") outside [his official residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Aghion "Beit Aghion") in Jerusalem on 30 July 2020

In February 2018, Israeli police recommended that Netanyahu be charged with corruption. According to a police statement, sufficient evidence exists to indict the prime minister on charges of bribery, fraud, and breach of trust in the two cases. Netanyahu responded that the allegations were baseless and that he would continue as prime minister.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbc_13_feb_2018-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-bbc_13_feb_2018) In November 2018, it was reported that Economic Crimes Division Director Liat Ben-Ari recommended indictment for both cases.[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-235)

In 2018 Netanyahu was also investigated in "Case 4000", where he was suspected of giving regulatory favors to [Shaul Elovitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaul_Elovitch "Shaul Elovitch"), owner of [Bezeq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bezeq "Bezeq") telecommunication company, in exchange for positive publications in news website [Walla!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walla! "Walla!").[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-236)[&#8202;](#ref-back-237-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-237)[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbc_13_feb_2018-1)[\[232\]](#refnote-bbc_13_feb_2018)

In February 2019, the Israeli attorney general announced his intent to file indictments against Netanyahu on bribe and fraud charges in the three cases.[&#8202;](#ref-back-indictments-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-indictments)[&#8202;](#ref-back-toi21nov-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-toi21nov) Netanyahu was formally indicted on 21 November 2019.[&#8202;](#ref-back-240-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-240)[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-241)[&#8202;](#ref-back-242-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-242) If convicted, he could face up to 10 years in prison for bribery and a maximum of three years for fraud and breach of trust.[&#8202;](#ref-back-243-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-243)[&#8202;](#ref-back-244-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-244) He is the first sitting prime minister in Israel's history to be charged with a crime.[&#8202;](#ref-back-toi21nov-1)[\[237\]](#refnote-toi21nov)[&#8202;](#ref-back-haaretznov23-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-haaretznov23)[&#8202;](#ref-back-toinov23-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-toinov23) On 23 November 2019, it was announced that Netanyahu, in compliance with legal precedent set by the Israeli Supreme Court in 1993,[&#8202;](#ref-back-haaretznov23-1)[\[243\]](#refnote-haaretznov23) would relinquish his agriculture, health, social affairs and diaspora affairs portfolios.[&#8202;](#ref-back-haaretznov23-2)[\[243\]](#refnote-haaretznov23)[&#8202;](#ref-back-toinov23-1)[\[244\]](#refnote-toinov23) The matter of forcing a prime minister to resign due to an indictment has yet to be tested in court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-haaretznov23-3)[\[243\]](#refnote-haaretznov23)[&#8202;](#ref-back-toinov23-2)[\[244\]](#refnote-toinov23) He was officially charged on 28 January 2020.[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-247)

Netanyahu's criminal trial was set to begin on 24 May 2020, having been initially scheduled for March of that year but delayed due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic").[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-248) As of April 2023, [the criminal trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Benjamin_Netanyahu "Trial of Benjamin Netanyahu") was still ongoing.[&#8202;](#ref-back-249-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-249)

On 30 November 2025, Netanyahu formally asked for a pardon from president [Isaac Herzog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaac_Herzog "Isaac Herzog").[&#8202;](#ref-back-250-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-250)

In February 2026, the Attorney General and Cabinet Legal Advisor of Israel summoned Netanyahu to provide explanations to the police regarding an investigation into the [leak of classified documents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_secret_document_leak_scandal "2024 Israeli secret document leak scandal") to the German newspaper [Bild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bild "Bild"). The case reportedly concerns attempts to obstruct investigations into the leak of sensitive Israeli documents. Previously, Netanyahu's chief of staff, [Tzachi Braverman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tzachi_Braverman "Tzachi Braverman"), was arrested on suspicion of attempting to impede the investigative process regarding the leak to foreign media.[&#8202;](#ref-back-251-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-251)[&#8202;](#ref-back-252-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-252)[&#8202;](#ref-back-253-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-253)

#### Fifth term

Further information: [Thirty-fifth government of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-fifth_government_of_Israel "Thirty-fifth government of Israel")

On 17 May 2020, Netanyahu was sworn in for a fifth term as prime minister in a coalition with [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz "Benny Gantz"). Against a background of the [COVID-19 pandemic in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Israel "COVID-19 pandemic in Israel") and Netanyahu's criminal trial, [protests broke out against him](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Benjamin_Netanyahu "Protests against Benjamin Netanyahu") in front of the prime minister's residence. Following this, Netanyahu ordered to disperse the demonstrations using COVID-19 special regulations, limiting them to 20 people and at a distance of 1,000 meters from their homes.[&#8202;](#ref-back-254-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-254) However, the exact opposite was achieved; the demonstrations were enlarged and dispersed to over 1,000 centers.[&#8202;](#ref-back-255-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-255) By March 2021, Israel became the country with the [highest vaccinated population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_vaccination_in_Israel "COVID-19 vaccination in Israel") per capita in the world against COVID-19.[&#8202;](#ref-back-256-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-256)

After tensions escalated in Jerusalem in May 2021, Hamas fired rockets on Israel from Gaza, which prompted Netanyahu to initiate [Operation Guardian of the Walls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_crisis "2021 Israel–Palestine crisis"), lasting eleven days.[&#8202;](#ref-back-257-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-257) After the operation, Israeli politician and leader of the Yamina alliance [Naftali Bennett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naftali_Bennett "Naftali Bennett") announced that he had agreed to a deal with Leader of the Opposition [Yair Lapid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yair_Lapid "Yair Lapid") to form a [rotation government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotation_government "Rotation government") that would oust Netanyahu from his position as prime minister.[&#8202;](#ref-back-258-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-258)[&#8202;](#ref-back-259-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-259) On 13 June 2021, Bennett and Lapid formed [a coalition government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-sixth_government_of_Israel "Thirty-sixth government of Israel"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-260) and Netanyahu was ousted as prime minister, ending his 12-year tenure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-261-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-261)[&#8202;](#ref-back-swornc-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-swornc)

### Leader of the Opposition (2021–2022)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [![[icon]](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/40px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg) | This section **needs expansion**. You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Benjamin_Netanyahu&action=edit&section=)[adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Benjamin_Netanyahu&action=edit&section=) or [making an edit request](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Requests_for_page_protection/Edit). _(August 2025)_ |

After the end of his second premiership, Netanyahu began his third stint as the leader of the opposition. Likud remained the largest party in [the twenty-fourth Knesset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_members_of_the_twenty-fourth_Knesset "List of members of the twenty-fourth Knesset").[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-263)
He led the opposition into the [2022 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Israeli_legislative_election "2022 Israeli legislative election").

### Prime Minister (2022–present)

#### Sixth term

Further information: [Thirty-seventh government of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-seventh_government_of_Israel "Thirty-seventh government of Israel") and [Israeli war cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_cabinet "Israeli war cabinet")

See also: [2023 Israeli judicial reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform "2023 Israeli judicial reform"), [2023 Israeli judicial reform protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform_protests "2023 Israeli judicial reform protests"), and [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/%D7%94%D7%9E%D7%97%D7%90%D7%94_%D7%A0%D7%92%D7%93_%D7%94%D7%94%D7%A4%D7%99%D7%9B%D7%94_%D7%94%D7%9E%D7%A9%D7%A4%D7%98%D7%99%D7%AA%2C_%D7%A7%D7%A4%D7%9C%D7%9F_%D7%A4%D7%99%D7%A0%D7%AA_%D7%9E%D7%A0%D7%97%D7%9D_%D7%91%D7%92%D7%99%D7%9F%2C_4_%D7%91%D7%9E%D7%A8%D7%A5_2023.jpg/500px-%D7%94%D7%9E%D7%97%D7%90%D7%94_%D7%A0%D7%92%D7%93_%D7%94%D7%94%D7%A4%D7%99%D7%9B%D7%94_%D7%94%D7%9E%D7%A9%D7%A4%D7%98%D7%99%D7%AA%2C_%D7%A7%D7%A4%D7%9C%D7%9F_%D7%A4%D7%99%D7%A0%D7%AA_%D7%9E%D7%A0%D7%97%D7%9D_%D7%91%D7%92%D7%99%D7%9F%2C_4_%D7%91%D7%9E%D7%A8%D7%A5_2023.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:%D7%94%D7%9E%D7%97%D7%90%D7%94_%D7%A0%D7%92%D7%93_%D7%94%D7%94%D7%A4%D7%99%D7%9B%D7%94_%D7%94%D7%9E%D7%A9%D7%A4%D7%98%D7%99%D7%AA,_%D7%A7%D7%A4%D7%9C%D7%9F_%D7%A4%D7%99%D7%A0%D7%AA_%D7%9E%D7%A0%D7%97%D7%9D_%D7%91%D7%92%D7%99%D7%9F,_4_%D7%91%D7%9E%D7%A8%D7%A5_2023.jpg) [Protest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform_protests "2023 Israeli judicial reform protests") against the [judicial reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform "2023 Israeli judicial reform") in March 2023[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Prime_Minister_Sunak_met_with_Prime_Minister_Netanyahu_at_10_Downing_Street_in_2023.jpg/500px-Prime_Minister_Sunak_met_with_Prime_Minister_Netanyahu_at_10_Downing_Street_in_2023.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Sunak_met_with_Prime_Minister_Netanyahu_at_10_Downing_Street_in_2023.jpg) Netanyahu with British prime minister [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak "Rishi Sunak") in London, 24 March 2023

After the [2022 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Israeli_legislative_election "2022 Israeli legislative election"), Netanyahu was sworn in as prime minister again as the leader of a hardline coalition.[&#8202;](#ref-back-264-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-264) He started his sixth term on 29 December 2022.[&#8202;](#ref-back-swornc-1)[\[260\]](#refnote-swornc)

The first months of Netanyahu's sixth term centered around [proposed reform to the judicial branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform "2023 Israeli judicial reform"). Critics highlighted the negative effects it would have on the [separation of powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_system_of_government#Separation_of_Powers "Israeli system of government"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-265-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-265)[&#8202;](#ref-back-266-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-266)[&#8202;](#ref-back-267-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-267) the office of the [Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_Israel "Attorney General of Israel"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-268-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-268)[&#8202;](#ref-back-269-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-269)[&#8202;](#ref-back-270-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-270) the economy,[&#8202;](#ref-back-271-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-271)[&#8202;](#ref-back-272-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-272)[&#8202;](#ref-back-273-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-273) public health,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Izak-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-Izak)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Barnea-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-Barnea) women and minorities,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Barnea-1)[\[273\]](#refnote-Barnea)[&#8202;](#ref-back-276-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-276) workers' rights,[&#8202;](#ref-back-277-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-277) scientific research,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Barnea-2)[\[273\]](#refnote-Barnea)[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-278) the strength of Israel's democracy[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-279)[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-280) and its foreign relations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-281-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-281) After [public protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform_protests "2023 Israeli judicial reform protests"), joined by [military reservists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_reserve_force "Military reserve force"), Minister of Defense [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant "Yoav Gallant") spoke against the reform on 25 March, calling for a halt "for the sake of Israel's security".[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-282) Netanyahu announced his intention to remove Gallant, sparking further protests and leading to Netanyahu agreeing to delay the legislation for a month.[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-283)[&#8202;](#ref-back-284-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-284)\[ _[needs update](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\]

In February 2023, the government approved the legalization of nine [settler outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost "Israeli outpost") in the occupied West Bank.[&#8202;](#ref-back-285-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-285) Finance Minister [Bezalel Smotrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bezalel_Smotrich "Bezalel Smotrich") took charge of most of the [Civil Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Civil_Administration "Israeli Civil Administration"), obtaining broad authority over civilian issues in the West Bank. Israeli peace groups condemned the move as _de jure_ [annexation of the occupied territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_Israeli_annexation_of_the_West_Bank "Proposed Israeli annexation of the West Bank").[&#8202;](#ref-back-286-0)[\[284\]](#refnote-286)[&#8202;](#ref-back-287-0)[\[285\]](#refnote-287)[&#8202;](#ref-back-NYT-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-NYT) In March, Netanyahu's government repealed a 2005 law whereby four Israeli settlements, [Homesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homesh "Homesh"), [Sa-Nur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sa-Nur "Sa-Nur"), [Ganim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganim "Ganim") and [Kadim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadim "Kadim"), were dismantled as part of the [Israeli disengagement from Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza "Israeli disengagement from Gaza").[&#8202;](#ref-back-289-0)[\[287\]](#refnote-289) In June, Netanyahu's coalition shortened the procedure of approving settlement construction, and gave Smotrich authority to approve one of the stages, changing the system operating for the previous 27 years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-290-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-290) In its first six months, construction of 13,000 housing units in settlements, almost triple the amount advanced in the whole of 2022.[&#8202;](#ref-back-291-0)[\[289\]](#refnote-291)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/P20230920CS-0125_%2853234992098%29.jpg/500px-P20230920CS-0125_%2853234992098%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:P20230920CS-0125_(53234992098).jpg) Netanyahu with U.S. president [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") in New York City, 20 September 2023

Israel refused to [send lethal weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War "List of military aid to Ukraine during the Russo-Ukrainian War") to [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine "Ukraine"). In June 2023, Netanyahu said Israel is concerned "with the possibility that systems that we would give to Ukraine would fall into Iranian hands and could be reverse engineered, and we would find ourselves facing Israeli systems used against Israel."[&#8202;](#ref-back-292-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-292)

On 7 October 2023, after Palestinian militants from Gaza launched [a major surprise attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_7_attacks "October 7 attacks"), Netanyahu announced that Israel would enter a war against Hamas.[&#8202;](#ref-back-293-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-293) He threatened to "turn all the places where Hamas is organized and hiding into cities of ruins", called [Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_City "Gaza City") "the city of evil", and urged its residents to "leave now".[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-294) He proposed opposition parties Yesh Atid and National Unity enter [an emergency unity government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_cabinet "Israeli war cabinet"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[293\]](#refnote-295) after Leader of the Opposition Lapid urged Netanyahu put "aside our differences and form an emergency, narrow, professional government."[&#8202;](#ref-back-296-0)[\[294\]](#refnote-296)

Netanyahu was criticized for presiding over Israel's biggest intelligence failure in 50 years, and has [faced protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel#%22Bring_Them_Home_Now%22_protests "Gaza war protests in Israel") calling for his removal. The war led to increased opposition to Netanyahu due to failure to anticipate the Hamas-led attack, with increased calls for Netanyahu's resignation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-297-0)[\[295\]](#refnote-297)[&#8202;](#ref-back-298-0)[\[296\]](#refnote-298)[&#8202;](#ref-back-299-0)[\[297\]](#refnote-299) A 2023 poll showed that 56% of Israelis believed Netanyahu must resign after the war, with 86% holding the country's leadership responsible for the security failings that led to the attack.[&#8202;](#ref-back-300-0)[\[298\]](#refnote-300)[&#8202;](#ref-back-301-0)[\[299\]](#refnote-301)[&#8202;](#ref-back-302-0)[\[300\]](#refnote-302)[&#8202;](#ref-back-303-0)[\[301\]](#refnote-303) On 28 October, Netanyahu was accused of using "dangerous rhetoric" when comparing Hamas to [Amalek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amalek "Amalek"), stating: "You must remember what Amalek has done to you, says our Holy Bible. And we do remember."[&#8202;](#ref-back-304-0)[\[302\]](#refnote-304) On 29 October, Netanyahu blamed Israel's security chiefs for Hamas's attack in a post on X; this was deleted following criticism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-305-0)[\[303\]](#refnote-305) An investigation carried out by [Shin Bet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shin_Bet "Shin Bet") acknowledged the agency's failures, but found that Shin Bet warned the prime minister that Hamas was not deterred, and objected to the divide-and-rule policy vis-a-vis Hamas and the Palestinian Authority.[&#8202;](#ref-back-shabak-0)[\[304\]](#refnote-shabak)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Benjamin_Netanyahu_addresses_the_118th_United_States_Congress_%28cropped%29.jpg/500px-Benjamin_Netanyahu_addresses_the_118th_United_States_Congress_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Benjamin_Netanyahu_addresses_the_118th_United_States_Congress_(cropped).jpg) Netanyahu addresses a joint session of Congress in Washington, D.C. with House Speaker [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson "Mike Johnson") and Senator [Ben Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Cardin "Ben Cardin"), 24 July 2024.

In November, he rejected calls for a ceasefire and warned that Israel will "stand firm against the world if necessary." He said the [Israel Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Defense_Forces "Israel Defense Forces") would remain in Gaza "as long as necessary" and Israel would prevent the Palestinian Authority from returning to Gaza.[&#8202;](#ref-back-307-0)[\[305\]](#refnote-307) Netanyahu called allegations that Israel is breaking international law "hogwash" and described Palestinian [civilian casualties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Gaza_war "Casualties of the Gaza war") as "collateral damage".[&#8202;](#ref-back-308-0)[\[306\]](#refnote-308) In December 2023, he faced criticism during a meeting with released [Israeli hostages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_hostage_crisis "Gaza war hostage crisis"). One hostage accused him of putting politics "above the return of the kidnapped."[&#8202;](#ref-back-309-0)[\[307\]](#refnote-309) Netanyahu said Israel should support the "voluntary migration" of Palestinians from Gaza.[&#8202;](#ref-back-310-0)[\[308\]](#refnote-310)

Israel under Netanyahu has been accused of committing genocide in Gaza in the [_South Africa v. Israel_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel_(Genocide_Convention) "South Africa v. Israel (Genocide Convention)") case before the [International Court of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Court_of_Justice "International Court of Justice") in December 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-311-0)[\[309\]](#refnote-311)[&#8202;](#ref-back-312-0)[\[310\]](#refnote-312) In May 2024, [Karim Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Ahmad_Khan "Karim Ahmad Khan"), the [prosecutor of the International Criminal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prosecutor_of_the_International_Criminal_Court "Prosecutor of the International Criminal Court"), announced his intention to apply for an [arrest warrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrest_warrant "Arrest warrant") for Netanyahu on counts of alleged [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crime "War crime") and [crimes against humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimes_against_humanity "Crimes against humanity").[&#8202;](#ref-back-ICCAW-0)[\[311\]](#refnote-ICCAW)[&#8202;](#ref-back-ICCAW_CNN-0)[\[312\]](#refnote-ICCAW_CNN)

In July 2024, Netanyahu addressed another [joint session of the United States Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_session_of_the_United_States_Congress "Joint session of the United States Congress"), amidst widespread protest, to solicit support for the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war").[&#8202;](#ref-back-315-0)[\[313\]](#refnote-315) He called protesters " [useful idiots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Useful_idiot "Useful idiot")" and pledged a "total victory" in Gaza.[&#8202;](#ref-back-316-0)[\[314\]](#refnote-316) He met with 2024 Republican nominee for president [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") at his [Mar-a-Lago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mar-a-Lago "Mar-a-Lago") residence where he criticized Democratic nominee [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris "Kamala Harris") for vowing that she "will not be silent" about atrocities in Gaza.[&#8202;](#ref-back-317-0)[\[315\]](#refnote-317)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/U.S._Secretary_of_Defense_Pete_Hegseth_hosts_a_bilateral_exchange_with_Israeli_Prime_Minister_Benjamin_Netanyahu_at_the_Pentagon%2C_USA_on_February_5%2C_2025_-_9.jpg/500px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:U.S._Secretary_of_Defense_Pete_Hegseth_hosts_a_bilateral_exchange_with_Israeli_Prime_Minister_Benjamin_Netanyahu_at_the_Pentagon,_USA_on_February_5,_2025_-_9.jpg) Netanyahu and U.S. Secretary of Defense [Pete Hegseth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Hegseth "Pete Hegseth") at the Pentagon on 5 February 2025

In October 2024, a [drone attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drone_warfare "Drone warfare") believed to have originated from Lebanon [was made on Netanyahu's residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_drone_attack_on_Benjamin_Netanyahu's_residence "2024 drone attack on Benjamin Netanyahu's residence") in [Caesarea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesarea_(modern_town) "Caesarea (modern town)"). Netanyahu was not in the residence, and no casualties were reported.[&#8202;](#ref-back-318-0)[\[316\]](#refnote-318) Netanyahu accused [Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah "Hezbollah") of trying to assassinate him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-319-0)[\[317\]](#refnote-319)

On 5 November 2024, Netanyahu fired defense minister Gallant, who had advocated for a short-term diplomatic deal. The firing triggered protests throughout Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-320-0)[\[318\]](#refnote-320) On 21 November, the [International Criminal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court "International Criminal Court") issued arrest warrants for Netanyahu, Gallant and Hamas military commander [Mohammed Deif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Deif "Mohammed Deif") for [alleged war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Gaza_war "War crimes in the Gaza war") committed during the Gaza war.[&#8202;](#ref-back-321-0)[\[319\]](#refnote-321) Netanyahu described the ruling as "absurd and false lies" and said the decision is "antisemitic".[&#8202;](#ref-back-322-0)[\[320\]](#refnote-322)

On the night of 18 March 2025, Israel launched a [surprise attack on the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Israeli_attacks_on_the_Gaza_Strip "March 2025 Israeli attacks on the Gaza Strip"), effectively ending the [January 2025 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire "January 2025 Gaza war ceasefire").[&#8202;](#ref-back-323-0)[\[321\]](#refnote-323) Netanyahu was scheduled on 18 March to testify in [his corruption trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Benjamin_Netanyahu "Trial of Benjamin Netanyahu"), but as a result of the attacks, the proceedings were postponed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-324-0)[\[322\]](#refnote-324) In March 2025, Netanyahu fired [Ronen Bar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronen_Bar "Ronen Bar"), chief of [Shin Bet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shin_Bet "Shin Bet"), citing a loss of confidence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-325-0)[\[323\]](#refnote-325) Bar accused the government of firing him for investigating [Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar "Qatar")'s involvement and influence in the Prime Minister's Office, in a case known as the [Qatari connection affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatari_connection_affair "Qatari connection affair").[&#8202;](#ref-back-326-0)[\[324\]](#refnote-326)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/The_White_House_-_54437403521.jpg/500px-The_White_House_-_54437403521.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_White_House_-_54437403521.jpg) Netanyahu with President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") and [Leo Terrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Terrell "Leo Terrell"), the head of the Trump administration's [Task Force to Combat Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Task_Force_to_Combat_Anti-Semitism "Joint Task Force to Combat Anti-Semitism"), 7 April 2025

In May, Netanyahu stated that the destruction of homes in Gaza would lead to the [forced emigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_evacuations "Gaza Strip evacuations") of Palestinians.[&#8202;](#ref-back-327-0)[\[325\]](#refnote-327)

On 13 June, Netanyahu authorized airstrikes against Iran, marking the beginning of the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War "Twelve-Day War").[&#8202;](#ref-back-unprecedented-0)[\[326\]](#refnote-unprecedented) Within a week, Netanyahu publicly commented on the effects of the war: "Each of us bears a personal cost, and my family has not been exempt", as he cited that it was "the second time that my son Avner has cancelled a wedding due to missile threats."[&#8202;](#ref-back-329-0)[\[327\]](#refnote-329) In July, Netanyahu was criticized for deciding to increase Gaza aid distribution by some in his cabinet, who rejected the U.S. ceasefire proposal.[&#8202;](#ref-back-330-0)[\[328\]](#refnote-330)

In August 2025, Netanyahu said in an interview that he was on a "historic and spiritual mission" and "very" attached to the vision of [Greater Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Israel "Greater Israel"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-331-0)[\[329\]](#refnote-331) which includes the Palestinian territories and other Arab states.[&#8202;](#ref-back-332-0)[\[330\]](#refnote-332) On 21 September, Netanyahu rejected the existence of a Palestinian state west of the [Jordan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_River "Jordan River"), saying, "For years, I have prevented the establishment of this terrorist state facing tremendous pressures at home and abroad."[&#8202;](#ref-back-333-0)[\[331\]](#refnote-333) In Netanyahu's address to the [United Nations General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly "United Nations General Assembly"), he opposed international recognition of a Palestinian state. As Netanyahu commenced his speech, dozens of individuals exited the hall in protest.[&#8202;](#ref-back-334-0)[\[332\]](#refnote-334)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Prime_Minister_of_Bharat%2C_Shri_Narendra_Damodardas_Modi_is_welcomed_by_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel%2C_Mr._Benjamin_Netanyahu_on_his_arrival_to_Israel.jpg/500px-Prime_Minister_of_Bharat%2C_Shri_Narendra_Damodardas_Modi_is_welcomed_by_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel%2C_Mr._Benjamin_Netanyahu_on_his_arrival_to_Israel.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_of_Bharat,_Shri_Narendra_Damodardas_Modi_is_welcomed_by_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel,_Mr._Benjamin_Netanyahu_on_his_arrival_to_Israel.jpg) Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narendra_Modi "Narendra Modi") is welcomed by Netanyahu on his arrival in Israel, 25 February 2026

On 7 November, the Chief Public Prosecutor's Office in [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey") issued international arrest warrants for Netanyahu and 36 other Israeli officials, accusing them of genocide and crimes against humanity in Gaza.[&#8202;](#ref-back-335-0)[\[333\]](#refnote-335) The charges were based on Israel's attacks on civilians, hospitals, infrastructure and the [Global Sumud Flotilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Sumud_Flotilla "Global Sumud Flotilla"), which was intercepted by the Israeli navy while delivering aid to Gaza.[&#8202;](#ref-back-336-0)[\[334\]](#refnote-336)

On 26 December 2025, under Netanyahu's premiership, Israel became the first country to recognize [Somaliland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somaliland "Somaliland")—a breakaway region of [Somalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somalia "Somalia")—as an independent state.[&#8202;](#ref-back-337-0)[\[335\]](#refnote-337)

On 28 February 2026, Israel and the United States launched a [major military offensive against Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_war "2026 Iran war") with the stated goal of [inducing regime change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regime_change_efforts_in_the_2026_Iran_war "Regime change efforts in the 2026 Iran war").[&#8202;](#ref-back-338-0)[\[336\]](#refnote-338) According to multiple diplomatic sources, Netanyahu lobbied U.S. President Donald Trump to initiate the joint strikes.[&#8202;](#ref-back-339-0)[\[337\]](#refnote-339)[&#8202;](#ref-back-340-0)[\[338\]](#refnote-340)

In March 2026, after Israel dropped charges against Israeli soldiers accused of sexual assault at the [Sde Teiman detention camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sde_Teiman_detention_camp "Sde Teiman detention camp"), Netanyahu said "Israel must pursue its enemies – not its heroic fighters".[&#8202;](#ref-back-341-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-341)[&#8202;](#ref-back-342-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-342)[&#8202;](#ref-back-343-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-343)[&#8202;](#ref-back-344-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-344)

In April 2026, Netanyahu said "There is no [ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_war_ceasefire "2026 Iran war ceasefire") in Lebanon. We are [continuing to strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_April_2026_Lebanon_attacks "8 April 2026 Lebanon attacks") Hezbollah with full force, and we will not stop until we restore your security."[&#8202;](#ref-back-345-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-345)[&#8202;](#ref-back-346-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-346)[&#8202;](#ref-back-347-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-347)

In July 2026, speaking in an interview with [Channel 14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_14_(Israel) "Channel 14 (Israel)"), Netanyahu said that the Gaza Strip was "no longer" a military threat to Israel, and that his decision to strike Iran had been "to save us from annihilation by nuclear bombs that were already in their hands", the latter statement drawing criticism from political rivals [Gadi Eisenkot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadi_Eisenkot "Gadi Eisenkot") and Naftali Bennett, who disputed that Iran had ever possessed nuclear weapons.[&#8202;](#ref-back-348-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-348)[&#8202;](#ref-back-349-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-349)[&#8202;](#ref-back-350-0)[\[348\]](#refnote-350) In the same interview, Netanyahu cited his ongoing corruption trial as evidence for the need for further [judicial reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_reform "Judicial reform").[&#8202;](#ref-back-351-0)[\[349\]](#refnote-351)

On 8 September 2026, [Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz "Haaretz") published an exposé claiming that Netanyahu had received advanced warning from UAE President [Mohamed bin Zayed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_bin_Zayed_Al_Nahyan "Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan") of the 7 October attacks days before, but did not warn Israeli security chiefs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-352-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-352) In response, Netanyahu denied the reporting as an "absolute lie" and announced that he will file a lawsuit against Haaretz for allegedly committing [libel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libel "Libel") against him through this exposé.[&#8202;](#ref-back-353-0)[\[351\]](#refnote-353) Netanyahu secretly visited the UAE that month, and allegedly asked Mohamed bin Zayed to deny those reports of having received advanced warning.[&#8202;](#ref-back-354-0)[\[352\]](#refnote-354) On 24 September, at a United Nations assembly in New York City Netanyahu [denied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_denial "Gaza genocide denial") that his administration was committing genocide against the Palestinian people, claiming Israel's actions in the war were "the opposite of genocide".[&#8202;](#ref-back-355-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-355) Netanyahu verbally attacked the Mayor of New York City [Zohran Mamdani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zohran_Mamdani "Zohran Mamdani") for accusing him of genocide, in turn accusing Mamdani of being in allegiance with Hamas, asserting; "Israel didn’t commit genocide. Israel prevented genocide. And that’s exactly what Mr. Mamdani’s Hamas buddies would have done to us had we not stopped them."[&#8202;](#ref-back-356-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-356)[&#8202;](#ref-back-357-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-357)

On 3 November 2026, United States President Donald Trump announced that the United States would cease providing nearly 4 billion dollars in [aid to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations#Military_aid_and_procurement "Israel–United States military relations") by December 2026, and that this had been proposed by Netanyahu.[&#8202;](#ref-back-358-0)[\[356\]](#refnote-358) The announcement drew domestic criticism of Netanyahu, with [Yair Golan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yair_Golan "Yair Golan") saying "The cessation of American aid deals a severe blow to Israel’s security and the quality of life within the country, as the burden of funding defense needs at such a critical juncture will fall entirely upon the Israeli public," and that "A responsible Prime Minister would have fought to increase American aid, not dismantle it".[&#8202;](#ref-back-359-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-359)

## Political positions

Main article: [Political positions of Benjamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_positions_of_Benjamin_Netanyahu "Political positions of Benjamin Netanyahu")

| [Conservatism in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Israel "Conservatism in Israel") |
| [![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Emblem_of_Israel.svg/250px-Emblem_of_Israel.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Emblem_of_Israel.svg) |
