Netanyahu's tenures as prime minister have been marked by accusations of [authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism "Authoritarianism") and [democratic backsliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding "Democratic backsliding"), including efforts to overhaul the judicial system in 2023 that critics argued were intended to protect him from corruption charges.[&#8202;](#ref-back-360-0)[\[358\]](#refnote-360)[&#8202;](#ref-back-court2-0)[\[359\]](#refnote-court2) His 2022 coalition government was described as the most right-wing in Israeli history.[&#8202;](#ref-back-362-0)[\[360\]](#refnote-362) He has supported stricter immigration controls and has called for Israel to assume long-term security responsibility over Gaza following the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war").[&#8202;](#ref-back-363-0)[\[361\]](#refnote-363)

Netanyahu has opposed the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords "Oslo Accords") and was described by peace envoy [Dennis Ross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Ross "Dennis Ross") as having "no real interest in pursuing peace."[&#8202;](#ref-back-364-0)[\[362\]](#refnote-364) In 2001, he was recorded stating he would interpret the Oslo Accords in a way that would prevent a return to the 1967 borders.[&#8202;](#ref-back-washingtonpost2-0)[\[363\]](#refnote-washingtonpost2) He has advocated for an "economic peace" approach focusing on economic cooperation rather than political resolution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-haaretz_11-0)[\[364\]](#refnote-haaretz_11-21) For years, he backed Qatari payments to Gaza, stating in 2019 that supporting Hamas was part of a strategy to prevent Palestinian statehood.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Vox15Oct2-0)[\[365\]](#refnote-Vox15Oct2) He publicly supported the [Trump peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Trump_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_plan "2020 Trump Israel–Palestine plan") in 2020. Netanyahu also played a leading role in the [Abraham Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Accords "Abraham Accords"), normalizing relations with the UAE, Bahrain, Sudan, and Morocco.[&#8202;](#ref-back-368-0)[\[366\]](#refnote-368)

Netanyahu has described Iran as the greatest threat to Israel's existence, comparing it to Nazi Germany, and has been a prominent opponent of the [Iran nuclear deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_nuclear_deal "Iran nuclear deal").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Haaretz160420082-0)[\[367\]](#refnote-Haaretz160420082)[&#8202;](#ref-back-370-0)[\[368\]](#refnote-370) In 2025, he authorized airstrikes against Iran's nuclear facilities, and in 2026, he launched joint strikes with the U.S. that assassinated the Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Khamenei "Ali Khamenei") and other officials, starting a major regional war. He has framed these campaigns as opportunities for regime change in Iran.[&#8202;](#ref-back-371-0)[\[369\]](#refnote-371) Netanyahu has written extensively on counter-terrorism, arguing that democracies must balance civil liberties with security measures while treating terrorism as a form of totalitarianism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-372-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-372)

A proponent of free-market policies, Netanyahu introduced significant reforms as finance minister, including privatization, tax reductions, and welfare-to-work programs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Likud_Leaders_20152-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-Likud_Leaders_20152) Supporters credited these reforms with increasing foreign investment and economic growth, while critics argued that they contributed to greater economic inequality.[&#8202;](#ref-back-374-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-374) He supports equal rights for the [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT "LGBT") community and has spoken against racism and discrimination toward Ethiopian Israelis.[&#8202;](#ref-back-375-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-375)[&#8202;](#ref-back-376-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-376) In 2026, he supported [legislation applying the death penalty to Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_Penalty_for_Terrorists_Law "Death Penalty for Terrorists Law") convicted of terrorism.[&#8202;](#ref-back-377-0)[\[375\]](#refnote-377)

## International relations

### Public opinion

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Netanyahu_international_confidence_map.svg/500px-Netanyahu_international_confidence_map.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Netanyahu_international_confidence_map.svg) Confidence in Netanyahu, by country (Pew Research Center, Spring 2026)

In Spring 2026, [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center "Pew Research Center") found that majorities in 32 out of 36 countries surveyed held negative views of Netanyahu.[&#8202;](#ref-back-378-0)[\[c\]](#refnote-378) A median share of 69% did not have confidence in Netanyahu to do the right thing in world affairs, versus 21% that did.[&#8202;](#ref-back-pew-polls-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-pew-polls) The share in the United States without confidence in Netanyahu rose from 42% in March 2023 to 59% in March 2026,[&#8202;](#ref-back-380-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-380) with disapproval also rising in 13 out of 24 countries since 2025.[&#8202;](#ref-back-pew-polls-1)[\[376\]](#refnote-pew-polls)

Since 2023, Netanyahu has presided over a significant decline in [Israel's international reputation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Israel#International_opinion "Foreign relations of Israel").[&#8202;](#ref-back-381-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-381)[&#8202;](#ref-back-382-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-382)[&#8202;](#ref-back-pew-polls-2)[\[376\]](#refnote-pew-polls) In September 2026, the _[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz "Haaretz")_ editorial board stated that Netanyahu had led Israel's reputation in the United States to an unprecedented low,[&#8202;](#ref-back-383-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-383) and Shalom Yerushalmi wrote in _[The Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel "The Times of Israel")_ that Netanyahu had made Israel an "international pariah".[&#8202;](#ref-back-384-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-384) In October, [Ezra Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ezra_Klein "Ezra Klein") wrote in _[The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times "The New York Times")_ that the result of Netanyahu's leadership was that much of the world "now sees Israel the way Gazans see it".[&#8202;](#ref-back-385-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-385)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Americans_sympathies_in_the_Middle_East_situation.png/500px-Americans_sympathies_in_the_Middle_East_situation.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Americans_sympathies_in_the_Middle_East_situation.png) US sympathies have turned away from Israel under Netanyahu's leadership. Netanyahu has been Prime Minister since 2009, except for 18 months during 2021–22.

The Spring 2026 Pew study found that opinion of Israel was also negative in 32 out of 36 countries,[&#8202;](#ref-back-386-0)[\[d\]](#refnote-386) and since 2025 had declined in 13 out of 24, improving in only one (Greece).[&#8202;](#ref-back-pew-polls-3)[\[376\]](#refnote-pew-polls) In 2026, [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc. "Gallup, Inc.") found that for the first time Americans sympathized more with Palestinians than Israelis, with the Israeli share decreasing from 64% in 2018 to 36% in 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-387-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-387) Israel ranked last out of fifty countries in 2024 and 2025 in the [Anholt Nation Brands Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nation_branding#Anholt_Nation_Brands_Index "Nation branding"), conducted by [Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos "Ipsos") in 20 countries.[&#8202;](#ref-back-388-0)[\[e\]](#refnote-388) The 6% fall in 2025 was Israel's fastest decline in 20 years of measurement, and was accompanied by a widespread perception that Israel is an [illegitimate state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legitimacy_of_the_State_of_Israel "Legitimacy of the State of Israel").[&#8202;](#ref-back-389-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-389)

In July 2024, [YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov "YouGov") found that pluralities of 44% to 57% in seven European countries,[&#8202;](#ref-back-390-0)[\[f\]](#refnote-390) and 38% in the United States, supported the [International Criminal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court "International Criminal Court") (ICC) issuing an [arrest warrant for Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICC_arrest_warrants_for_Israeli_leaders "ICC arrest warrants for Israeli leaders") for war crimes and crimes against humanity in the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war"), with 30% in the US, and 15–24% in European countries, opposed. Large majorities in European countries of 62–74%, and 49% in the US, believed that the Netanyahu government had committed war crimes in Gaza, versus 8–12% and 26%, respectively, that did not.[&#8202;](#ref-back-391-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-391)

In July 2026, YouGov found that Americans thought by 49% to 27% that Netanyahu should be arrested if he entered the US, and by 43% to 24% that Israel was committing [genocide in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide "Gaza genocide").[&#8202;](#ref-back-392-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-392) In September 2025, _[The Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post "The Washington Post")_ found that 61% of [Jewish Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jews "American Jews") thought Israel had committed war crimes against Palestinians, while 29% did not, and 39% thought it had committed genocide, while 51% did not.[&#8202;](#ref-back-393-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-393)

### Foreign leaders

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Putin-Netanyahu-Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade_09-05-2018.jpg/500px-Putin-Netanyahu-Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade_09-05-2018.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Putin-Netanyahu-Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade_09-05-2018.jpg) Netanyahu with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin") at the [2018 Moscow Victory Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade "2018 Moscow Victory Day Parade"). The two leaders have had a close relationship.

Serving as prime minister in three nonconsecutive periods since the 1990s,[&#8202;](#ref-back-394-0)[\[388\]](#refnote-394)[&#8202;](#ref-back-395-0)[\[389\]](#refnote-395) Netanyahu has had relationships with various foreign leaders.

Netanyahu had a warm relationship and "personal friendship" with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin").[&#8202;](#ref-back-396-0)[\[390\]](#refnote-396)[&#8202;](#ref-back-397-0)[\[391\]](#refnote-397) In his 2022 book, Netanyahu wrote positively about Putin and describes him as "smart, sophisticated and focused on one goal – returning Russia to its historical greatness".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Netanyahu_Putin_Kuleba-0)[\[392\]](#refnote-Netanyahu_Putin_Kuleba) Their relationship has been strained since the start of the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war").[&#8202;](#ref-back-399-0)[\[393\]](#refnote-399)

Netanyahu and Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan "Recep Tayyip Erdoğan") have tense relations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-400-0)[\[394\]](#refnote-400) In March 2019, after being denounced by Turkey as a racist for saying that Israel was the nation-state of the Jewish people only, Netanyahu called Erdoğan a dictator and mocked him for imprisoning journalists in a tweet.[&#8202;](#ref-back-AP_Erdogan-0)[\[395\]](#refnote-AP_Erdogan) In response, Erdoğan called Netanyahu as "the thief who heads Israel", referencing the ongoing corruption scandals against Netanyahu. In the same speech, Erdoğan further escalated the spat by addressing to Netanyahu directly, saying, "you are a tyrant. You are a tyrant who slaughters 7-year-old Palestinian kids",[&#8202;](#ref-back-AP_Erdogan-1)[\[395\]](#refnote-AP_Erdogan) and further in April 2018, calling Israel "terror state" and Netanyahu "terrorist".[&#8202;](#ref-back-402-0)[\[396\]](#refnote-402) Netanyahu tweeted that "Erdoğan is among Hamas's biggest supporters and there is no doubt that he well understands terrorism and slaughter."[&#8202;](#ref-back-403-0)[\[397\]](#refnote-403) Netanyahu condemned the [2019 Turkish offensive into north-eastern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Turkish_offensive_into_north-eastern_Syria "2019 Turkish offensive into north-eastern Syria") and warned against ethnic cleansing of [Kurds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurds_in_Syria "Kurds in Syria") by Turkey and its proxies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Haaretz-0)[\[398\]](#refnote-Haaretz)

Netanyahu and Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narendra_Modi "Narendra Modi") developed a close relationship and [ties between India and Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Israel_relations "India–Israel relations") increased during their rule.[&#8202;](#ref-back-405-0)[\[399\]](#refnote-405)[&#8202;](#ref-back-406-0)[\[400\]](#refnote-406)[&#8202;](#ref-back-407-0)[\[401\]](#refnote-407)

Netanyahu has a close relationship with Hungarian prime minister [Viktor Orbán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Orb%C3%A1n "Viktor Orbán"), their having known each other for decades due to the privileged relationship between the Likud Party and the EPP, the [European People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_People's_Party "European People's Party"). Orban particularly admired Netanyahu while he was working as finance minister, and received advice from him while Netanyahu was Finance Minister of Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-408-0)[\[402\]](#refnote-408)

Netanyahu had a close and friendly relationship with Italian prime minister [Silvio Berlusconi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvio_Berlusconi "Silvio Berlusconi").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Haaretz20110614-0)[\[403\]](#refnote-Haaretz20110614) Netanyahu has said of Berlusconi: "We are lucky that there is a leader such as yourself."[&#8202;](#ref-back-410-0)[\[404\]](#refnote-410) Netanyahu has described Berlusconi as "one of the greatest friends".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Haaretz20110614-1)[\[403\]](#refnote-Haaretz20110614)[&#8202;](#ref-back-telegraph.co.uk-0)[\[405\]](#refnote-telegraph.co.uk)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Budapeszt_Spotkanie_premier%C3%B3w_pa%C5%84stw_Grupy_Wyszehradzkiej_%2835181214174%29.jpg/500px-Budapeszt_Spotkanie_premier%C3%B3w_pa%C5%84stw_Grupy_Wyszehradzkiej_%2835181214174%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Budapeszt_Spotkanie_premier%C3%B3w_pa%C5%84stw_Grupy_Wyszehradzkiej_(35181214174).jpg) Netanyahu with [Viktor Orbán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Orb%C3%A1n "Viktor Orbán") and other [V4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group "Visegrád Group") leaders at the V4-Israel summit in Budapest, Hungary on 19 July 2017[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/The_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Narendra_Modi_welcomes_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel%2C_Mr._Benjamin_Netanyahu%2C_on_his_arrival%2C_at_Air_Force_Station%2C_Palam%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_January_14%2C_2018_%284%29.jpg/500px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Narendra_Modi_welcomes_the_Prime_Minister_of_Israel,_Mr._Benjamin_Netanyahu,_on_his_arrival,_at_Air_Force_Station,_Palam,_in_New_Delhi_on_January_14,_2018_(4).jpg) Netanyahu and Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narendra_Modi "Narendra Modi") in New Delhi, India on 14 January 2018. The two leaders have had a close relationship.[&#8202;](#ref-back-412-0)[\[406\]](#refnote-412)

In early 2018, the Polish parliament adopted a [new Polish law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amendment_to_the_Act_on_the_Institute_of_National_Remembrance "Amendment to the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance") criminalizing suggestions that Poles were collectively complicit in [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust "Holocaust")-related or other war crimes that had been committed during [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II "World War II") by the [Axis powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_powers "Axis powers").[&#8202;](#ref-back-413-0)[\[407\]](#refnote-413)[&#8202;](#ref-back-414-0)[\[408\]](#refnote-414) Later that year at the Munich Security Conference, Polish prime minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mateusz_Morawiecki "Mateusz Morawiecki") said "it is not going to be seen as criminal to say that there were Polish perpetrators, as there were Jewish perpetrators... not only German perpetrators" implicated in the Jewish Holocaust.[&#8202;](#ref-back-415-0)[\[409\]](#refnote-415) Netanyahu called his Polish counterpart's comment "outrageous" for saying that Jews had been among the Holocaust's perpetrators.[&#8202;](#ref-back-416-0)[\[410\]](#refnote-416) The resulting crisis in [Israel–Poland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Poland_relations "Israel–Poland relations") was resolved in late June that year when the two prime ministers issued a joint communiqué endorsing research into the Jewish Holocaust and condemning the misnomer " [Polish concentration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%22Polish_death_camp%22_controversy "\"Polish death camp\" controversy")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-417-0)[\[411\]](#refnote-417)

According to [Efraim Zuroff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efraim_Zuroff "Efraim Zuroff") of the [Simon Wiesenthal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Wiesenthal_Center "Simon Wiesenthal Center"), during the visit of [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine "Ukraine") President [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko "Petro Poroshenko") in Jerusalem, Netanyahu failed to publicly address [Ukraine's official policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_decommunization_laws "Ukrainian decommunization laws") of rehabilitating local Nazi collaborators like [UPA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Insurgent_Army "Ukrainian Insurgent Army") leader [Roman Shukhevych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Shukhevych "Roman Shukhevych"), who had participated in the murder of Jews.[&#8202;](#ref-back-418-0)[\[412\]](#refnote-418)

Netanyahu had developed a close relationship with Brazilian president [Jair Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jair_Bolsonaro "Jair Bolsonaro") following Bolsonaro's [2018 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Brazilian_general_election "2026 Brazilian general election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-419-0)[\[413\]](#refnote-419)[&#8202;](#ref-back-420-0)[\[414\]](#refnote-420) Netanyahu endorsed Jair Bolsonaro's unsuccessful reelection bid in [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election "2022 Brazilian general election")[&#8202;](#ref-back-421-0)[\[415\]](#refnote-421) and later endorsed his son [Flávio Bolsonaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fl%C3%A1vio_Bolsonaro "Flávio Bolsonaro") in the [2026 Brazilian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Brazilian_general_election "2026 Brazilian general election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-422-0)[\[416\]](#refnote-422) Netanyahu has also developed a good relationship with Argentinian president [Javier Milei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Milei "Javier Milei"), having called him a "great friend of the Jewish State", shortly after Milei started his presidential tenure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-423-0)[\[417\]](#refnote-423)

Since 2023, Netanyahu and Chinese president [Xi Jinping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xi_Jinping "Xi Jinping") have been engaged in diplomacy, arising due to strained ties between the US and Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-424-0)[\[418\]](#refnote-424) The diplomatic situation has been made complicated due to the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war"), where China has remained neutral.[&#8202;](#ref-back-425-0)[\[419\]](#refnote-425)

### US leaders

Netanyahu's narrow victory in the [1996 Israeli general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Israeli_general_election "1996 Israeli general election") was seen as a rejection of the [Oslo process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords "Oslo Accords") under U.S. president [Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton "Bill Clinton"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-426-0)[\[420\]](#refnote-426) who had preferred Netanyahu's opponent, [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres "Shimon Peres"), in that election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-427-0)[\[421\]](#refnote-427) According to _[The Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel "The Times of Israel")_, Netanyahu "lectured" Clinton on the [Israel-Palestine conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict "Israeli–Palestinian conflict") when they first met in 1996, after which Clinton reportedly remarked in exasperation to aides: "Who's the fucking superpower here?"[&#8202;](#ref-back-428-0)[\[422\]](#refnote-428)

Netanyahu has close ties with the congressional leadership of the [U.S. Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_(United_States) "Republican Party (United States)") and with its 2012 presidential candidate, [Mitt Romney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney "Mitt Romney"). He and Romney first became acquainted when both worked at the [Boston Consulting Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Consulting_Group "Boston Consulting Group") in the mid-1970s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-429-0)[\[423\]](#refnote-429)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/P052011PS-0414_%286599543159%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/500px-P052011PS-0414_%286599543159%29_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:P052011PS-0414_(6599543159)_(cropped).jpg) Neyanyahu's relationship with [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") was tense, with disagreements over Iran and Israeli settlements.[&#8202;](#ref-back-430-0)[\[424\]](#refnote-430)[&#8202;](#ref-back-TibonShalev2015-0)[\[425\]](#refnote-TibonShalev2015)

Netanyahu's relationship with U.S. president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama") in the early years of the Obama administration were marked by disagreements over the construction of Israeli settlements in the West Bank.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TibonShalev2015-1)[\[425\]](#refnote-TibonShalev2015) During the [2011 G20 Cannes summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_G20_Cannes_summit "2011 G20 Cannes summit"), French president [Nicolas Sarkozy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Sarkozy "Nicolas Sarkozy") was overheard on a [hot mic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_mic "Hot mic") saying to U.S. president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama"), "I cannot bear Netanyahu, he's a liar", to which Obama responded, "You're fed up with him, but I have to deal with him every day."[&#8202;](#ref-back-YNet_Overheard-0)[\[426\]](#refnote-YNet_Overheard)[&#8202;](#ref-back-CBS_Fed_Up-0)[\[427\]](#refnote-CBS_Fed_Up) In October 2014, author [Jeffrey Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Goldberg "Jeffrey Goldberg") said that senior officials of the Obama administration had over a period of years called Netanyahu "recalcitrant, myopic, reactionary, obtuse, blustering, pompous" as well as "chickenshit", and said that Netanyahu "acts as though he is mayor of Jerusalem".[&#8202;](#ref-back-crisis-1)[\[179\]](#refnote-crisis) Secretary of State [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry "John Kerry") phoned Netanyahu to clarify that "such statements are disgraceful, unacceptable and damaging" and "do not reflect the position of the United States".[&#8202;](#ref-back-434-0)[\[428\]](#refnote-434) Netanyahu responded by saying "I'm being attacked because of my determination to defend Israel's interests. The safety of Israel is not important to those who attack me anonymously and personally."[&#8202;](#ref-back-435-0)[\[429\]](#refnote-435) Observers characterized the relationship with the Obama administration as having reached a crisis level by October 2014.[&#8202;](#ref-back-436-0)[\[430\]](#refnote-436)[&#8202;](#ref-back-crisis-2)[\[179\]](#refnote-crisis) Goldberg reported that Israel would wait until after the [2016 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election "2016 United States presidential election") before attempting to repair the relationship with the White House.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

Relations with Obama worsened when the U.S., as part of the [P5+1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P5+1 "P5+1"), began [negotiations for an Iran nuclear deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negotiations_leading_to_the_Iran_nuclear_deal "Negotiations leading to the Iran nuclear deal") after the [2013 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Iranian_presidential_election "2013 Iranian presidential election") of Iranian [reformist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_reformists "Iranian reformists") president [Hassan Rouhani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Rouhani "Hassan Rouhani"). Netayanu strongly opposed the [2015 Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action "Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action") (JCPOA), and gave a [speech to the U.S. Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu's_2015_address_to_the_United_States_Congress "Benjamin Netanyahu's 2015 address to the United States Congress") against it in March 2015, further weakening the relationship. The deal was finalized in July 2015.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TibonShalev2015-2)[\[425\]](#refnote-TibonShalev2015)

In December 2016, the U.S., under the Obama administration, abstained from the [United Nations Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council "United Nations Security Council") vote that passed [Resolution 2334](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2334 "United Nations Security Council Resolution 2334") calling for an end to [Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_settlement "Israeli settlement").[&#8202;](#ref-back-437-0)[\[431\]](#refnote-437) Previous U.S. presidents had [vetoed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_veto_power "United Nations Security Council veto power") such resolutions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Baker2016-0)[\[432\]](#refnote-Baker2016) At the behest of the Netanyahu government, President-elect Trump interceded by publicly advocating for the resolution to be vetoed, as well as persuading Egypt's [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdel_Fattah_el-Sisi "Abdel Fattah el-Sisi") to temporarily withdraw it from consideration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Baker2016-1)[\[432\]](#refnote-Baker2016) The resolution was then "proposed again by [Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia "Malaysia"), [New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand "New Zealand"), [Senegal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senegal "Senegal") and [Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuela "Venezuela")"– and passed 14 to 0. Netanyahu's office alleged that the Obama administration "not only failed to protect Israel against this gang-up at the UN, it colluded with it behind the scenes", adding it would work with President-elect Trump to negate the effects of the resolution.[&#8202;](#ref-back-439-0)[\[433\]](#refnote-439)[&#8202;](#ref-back-440-0)[\[434\]](#refnote-440)[&#8202;](#ref-back-441-0)[\[435\]](#refnote-441) According to [Alon Pinkas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alon_Pinkas "Alon Pinkas"), "Netanyahu's self-righteousness that this resolution is going to be changed or reversed by Trump is totally unfounded."[&#8202;](#ref-back-442-0)[\[436\]](#refnote-442) Deputy national security adviser [Benjamin J. Rhodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Rhodes_(White_House_staffer) "Ben Rhodes (White House staffer)") said that Netanyahu "had the opportunity to pursue policies that would have led to a different outcome today".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Baker2016-2)[\[432\]](#refnote-Baker2016)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/President_Trump_and_The_First_Lady_Participate_in_an_Abraham_Accords_Signing_Ceremony_%2850346326831%29.jpg/500px-President_Trump_and_The_First_Lady_Participate_in_an_Abraham_Accords_Signing_Ceremony_%2850346326831%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_Trump_and_The_First_Lady_Participate_in_an_Abraham_Accords_Signing_Ceremony_(50346326831).jpg) Netanyahu and [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") during the signing of the [Abraham Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Accords "Abraham Accords") on 15 September 2020

Netanyahu and U.S. president [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") have known each other for many years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-443-0)[\[437\]](#refnote-443) Netanyahu had been a friend of Donald Trump's father, [Fred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Trump "Fred Trump"), when Netanyahu lived in New York during the 1980s, serving as UN ambassador.[&#8202;](#ref-back-nymag.com-1)[\[43\]](#refnote-nymag.com) In 2013, Trump made a video endorsing Netanyahu during the [Israeli elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Israeli_legislative_election "2013 Israeli legislative election") saying, "vote for Benjamin – terrific guy, terrific leader, great for Israel".[&#8202;](#ref-back-444-0)[\[438\]](#refnote-444) In June 2019, Netanyahu officially renamed a [settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_Heights "Trump Heights") in the disputed [Golan Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights "Golan Heights") after Donald Trump.[&#8202;](#ref-back-445-0)[\[439\]](#refnote-445)[&#8202;](#ref-back-446-0)[\[440\]](#refnote-446) However, Trump aide [Jared Kushner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jared_Kushner "Jared Kushner") has claimed that in January 2020, Trump became frustrated with Netanyahu's rhetoric regarding [annexation of the Jordan Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_Israeli_annexation_of_the_West_Bank "Proposed Israeli annexation of the West Bank"), and considered endorsing his political opponent, [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz "Benny Gantz").[&#8202;](#ref-back-447-0)[\[441\]](#refnote-447) Following Netanyahu's congratulations for [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") after the [2020 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_U.S._presidential_election "2020 U.S. presidential election"), the relationship deteriorated, with Trump accusing him of disloyalty and stating Netanyahu had "made a terrible mistake".[&#8202;](#ref-back-448-0)[\[442\]](#refnote-448)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Isaac_Herzog_at_Ben_Gurion_Airport%2C_October_2023_%28ABG_4491%29.jpg/500px-Isaac_Herzog_at_Ben_Gurion_Airport%2C_October_2023_%28ABG_4491%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Isaac_Herzog_at_Ben_Gurion_Airport,_October_2023_(ABG_4491).jpg) Netanyahu with [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") and [Isaac Herzog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaac_Herzog "Isaac Herzog"), 18 October 2023

U.S. president [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden"), a [Democrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_(United_States) "Democratic Party (United States)"), has been friendly with Netanyahu since they first met in 1982 when Netanyahu was deputy chief of the [Israeli Embassy in Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Israel,_Washington,_D.C. "Embassy of Israel, Washington, D.C.").[&#8202;](#ref-back-449-0)[\[443\]](#refnote-449) In November 2011[&#8202;](#ref-back-450-0)[\[444\]](#refnote-450) and in the [2012 U.S. vice presidential debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_election_debates,_2012 "United States presidential election debates, 2012"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-451-0)[\[445\]](#refnote-451) Biden stated that the relationship had lasted for 39 years. In March 2010, Netanyahu remarked during a joint statement with Biden during his visit Israel that their friendship had started almost three decades prior.[&#8202;](#ref-back-452-0)[\[446\]](#refnote-452) During Spring 2024, the relationship between the two leaders had become strained over Israel's [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive "Rafah offensive"), with Biden privately calling Netanyahu a "fucking liar".[&#8202;](#ref-back-453-0)[\[447\]](#refnote-453)

Netanyahu and Trump repaired their relationship in July 2024 amid the lead-up to the [2024 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election "2024 United States presidential election"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-454-0)[\[448\]](#refnote-454) in which Netanyahu endorsed Trump.[&#8202;](#ref-back-455-0)[\[449\]](#refnote-455) Netanyahu congratulated Trump on his election to a [second presidential term](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_presidency_of_Donald_Trump "Second presidency of Donald Trump") as "history's greatest comeback,"[&#8202;](#ref-back-456-0)[\[450\]](#refnote-456) and later asserted that Trump was "the greatest friend that Israel has ever had in the White House."[&#8202;](#ref-back-457-0)[\[451\]](#refnote-457) Together they proposed the [February 2025 Gaza Strip takeover plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_Gaza_Strip_takeover_proposal "2025 Donald Trump Gaza Strip takeover proposal") and the [October 2025 Gaza peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_peace_plan "Gaza peace plan") and attacked Iran in the 2025 [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War "Twelve-Day War") and [2026 Iran war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_war "2026 Iran war").[&#8202;](#ref-back-458-0)[\[452\]](#refnote-458) In June 2026, [_Axios_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axios_(website) "Axios (website)") reported that Trump swore at Netanyahu during a phone conversation after Israel resumed [bombing Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Lebanon_war "2026 Lebanon war"), telling Netanyahu: "You'd be in prison if it weren't for me. I'm saving your ass. Everybody hates you now. Everybody hates Israel because of this."[&#8202;](#ref-back-459-0)[\[453\]](#refnote-459) He had also expressed anger at Israel, alongside Iran, in the closing stages of the 2025 war.[&#8202;](#ref-back-460-0)[\[454\]](#refnote-460) Trump endorsed Netanyahu ahead of the [2026 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Israeli_legislative_election "2026 Israeli legislative election"), declaring that any other candidate would be the "wrong leader."[&#8202;](#ref-back-461-0)[\[455\]](#refnote-461)

### Marriages and relationships

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_P.M._BENJAMIN_NETANYAHU_LIGHTING_HANUKA_CANDLES_WITH_HIS_WIFE_AND_SONS.jpg/500px-Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_%28GPO%29_-_P.M._BENJAMIN_NETANYAHU_LIGHTING_HANUKA_CANDLES_WITH_HIS_WIFE_AND_SONS.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flickr_-_Government_Press_Office_(GPO)_-_P.M._BENJAMIN_NETANYAHU_LIGHTING_HANUKA_CANDLES_WITH_HIS_WIFE_AND_SONS.jpg) Netanyahu lighting [Hanukkah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanukkah "Hanukkah") [candles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menorah_(Hanukkah) "Menorah (Hanukkah)") on the first night in the [prime minister's office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Aghion "Beit Aghion") in [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem "Jerusalem") with his wife, [Sara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Netanyahu "Sara Netanyahu") and their sons, [Yair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yair_Netanyahu "Yair Netanyahu") and Avner, 1996

Netanyahu has been married three times and has been involved in multiple [extramarital affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affair "Affair"). Netanyahu's first marriage was to Miriam Weizmann, whom he met in Israel. Weizmann lived near Yonatan Netanyahu's apartment in Jerusalem, where Netanyahu was based during his military service. By the time Netanyahu's service was finished, Weizmann had completed her own military service as well as a degree in chemistry from the [Hebrew University of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University_of_Jerusalem "Hebrew University of Jerusalem"). In 1972, they both left to study in the United States, where she enrolled in [Brandeis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandeis_University "Brandeis University"), while Netanyahu studied at MIT. They married soon afterward. The couple had one daughter, Noa (born 29 April 1978).[&#8202;](#ref-back-Haaretz-1)[\[456\]](#refnote-Haaretz)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Miskin-0)[\[457\]](#refnote-Miskin)

In 1978, while Weizmann was pregnant, Netanyahu met a non-Jewish British student named Fleur Cates at the university library, and began an affair. His marriage ended in divorce soon after Miriam discovered the affair. In 1981, Netanyahu married Cates, and she [converted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conversion_to_Judaism "Conversion to Judaism") to [Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism "Judaism").[&#8202;](#ref-back-star-0)[\[458\]](#refnote-star) After moving with Netanyahu to Israel, Cates sued for divorce in 1988.[&#8202;](#ref-back-star-1)[\[458\]](#refnote-star)

His third wife, [Sara Ben-Artzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Netanyahu "Sara Netanyahu"), was working as a [flight attendant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_attendant "Flight attendant") on an [El Al](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Al "El Al") flight from New York to Israel when they met.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bio-1)[\[74\]](#refnote-bio) She was in the process of completing a master's degree in psychology.[&#8202;](#ref-back-465-0)[\[459\]](#refnote-465) The couple married in 1991. They have two sons: [Yair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yair_Netanyahu "Yair Netanyahu") (born 26 July 1991), a former soldier in the [IDF Spokesperson's Unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IDF_Spokesperson's_Unit "IDF Spokesperson's Unit"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-466-0)[\[460\]](#refnote-466) and Avner (born 10 October 1994), a national Bible champion, winner of the National Bible Quiz for Youth in [Kiryat Shmona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Shmona "Kiryat Shmona"), and former soldier in the IDF [Combat Intelligence Collection Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_Intelligence_Collection_Corps "Combat Intelligence Collection Corps").[&#8202;](#ref-back-467-0)[\[461\]](#refnote-467)[&#8202;](#ref-back-468-0)[\[462\]](#refnote-468)

In 1993, Netanyahu confessed on live television to having an affair with Ruth Bar, his public relations adviser. He stated that a political rival had planted a [secret video camera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hidden_camera "Hidden camera") that recorded him in a sexually compromising position with Bar and that he had been [threatened with the release of the tape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sextortion "Sextortion") to the press unless he withdrew from the Likud leadership race. Netanyahu and Sara repaired their marriage, and he was subsequently elected to the leadership of Likud.[&#8202;](#ref-back-469-0)[\[463\]](#refnote-469)

### Health

Netanyahu has been suffering from [right bundle branch block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_bundle_branch_block "Right bundle branch block") (RBBB) since around 2003.[&#8202;](#ref-back-470-0)[\[464\]](#refnote-470) On 22 July 2023, a pacemaker was implanted in his body.[&#8202;](#ref-back-471-0)[\[465\]](#refnote-471)[&#8202;](#ref-back-472-0)[\[466\]](#refnote-472) In the first half of 2008, doctors removed a small [colon polyp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colon_polyp "Colon polyp") that proved to be benign.[&#8202;](#ref-back-473-0)[\[467\]](#refnote-473) A hernia was repaired in March 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-474-0)[\[468\]](#refnote-474) In December 2024, his prostate was removed following a urinary tract infection caused by enlargement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-475-0)[\[469\]](#refnote-475)

His personal physician and close friend is the Romanian-born pediatrist [Herman Berkovits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herman_Berkovits "Herman Berkovits").[&#8202;](#ref-back-476-0)[\[470\]](#refnote-476)

Netanyahu was falsely reported as dead in March 2026, and subsequently posted a video mocking the claims, which was verified by [Reuters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reuters "Reuters").[&#8202;](#ref-back-477-0)[\[471\]](#refnote-477) According to [i24NEWS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I24NEWS_(Israeli_TV_channel) "I24NEWS (Israeli TV channel)"), the rumor was started with a false report by Iranian [Tasnim News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasnim_News_Agency "Tasnim News Agency").[&#8202;](#ref-back-478-0)[\[472\]](#refnote-478)

In April 2026, Netanyahu announced that he had been treated for early-stage [prostate cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prostate_cancer "Prostate cancer").[&#8202;](#ref-back-479-0)[\[473\]](#refnote-479) The [Israeli Prime Minister's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister's_Office_(Israel) "Prime Minister's Office (Israel)") disclosed that the surgery he had undergone in December 2024, was for cancer, not an infection as had been previously reported.[&#8202;](#ref-back-480-0)[\[474\]](#refnote-480)

In August 2026, Netanyahu claimed that the Iranian regime attempted to murder one of his children.[&#8202;](#ref-back-481-0)[\[475\]](#refnote-481)

3. [&#8202;](#refnote-378)[↑](#ref-back-378-0)The 36 countries surveyed were Argentina, Australia, Bangladesh, Brazil, Canada, Chile, Colombia, France, Germany, Ghana, Greece, Hungary, India, Indonesia, Italy, Japan, Kenya, Malaysia, Mexico, Netherlands, Nigeria, Pakistan, Peru, Philippines, Poland, Singapore, South Africa, South Korea, Spain, Sri Lanka, Sweden, Thailand, Turkey, the United Kingdom, the United States and the West Bank/East Jerusalem.

Only Kenya, India and the Philippines held positive views of Netanyahu. Ghana was split evenly. Except in Nigeria and Sri Lanka, all other countries had an absolute majority without confidence in Netanyahu.
4. [&#8202;](#refnote-386)[↑](#ref-back-386-0)Only Kenya, Nigeria, Ghana and India held positive views of Israel, and all other countries except Sri Lanka had an majority unfavorable to Israel.
5. [&#8202;](#refnote-388)[↑](#ref-back-388-0)Argentina, Australia, Brazil, Canada, China, France, Germany, India, Italy, Japan, Mexico, Poland, Russia, Saudi Arabia, South Africa, South Korea, Sweden, Turkey (Türkiye), the United Kingdom, and the United States
6. [&#8202;](#refnote-390)[↑](#ref-back-390-0)France, Germany, Spain, Italy, Sweden, Denmark and the United Kingdom.

The Commission has been investigating the events on and since 7 October 2023 for the last two years, and concluded that Israeli authorities and Israeli security forces committed four of the five genocidal acts defined by the 1948 Convention on the Prevention and Punishment of the Crime of Genocide, namely killing, causing serious bodily or mental harm, deliberately inflicting conditions of life calculated to bring about the destruction of the Palestinians in whole or in part, and imposing measures intended to prevent births.

Retrieved 22 December 2025. Para. 233: the Commission concludes that the 'direct and public incitement to commit genocide' under article III(c) of the Genocide Convention is established. Para. 252. The Commission concludes on reasonable grounds that the Israeli authorities and Israeli security forces have committed and are continuing to commit the following actus reus of genocide against the Palestinians in the Gaza Strip, namely (i) killing members of the group; (ii) causing serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group; (iii) deliberately inflicting on the group conditions of life calculated to bring about its physical destruction in whole or in part; and (iv) imposing measures intended to prevent births within the group
     - [""More than a human can bear": Israel's systematic use of sexual, reproductive and other forms of gender-based violence since 7 October 2023"](https://www.ohchr.org/sites/default/files/documents/hrbodies/hrcouncil/sessions-regular/session58/a-hrc-58-crp-6.pdf)(PDF). _UN Human Rights Council_. 13 March 2025. Retrieved 22 December 2025. Para. 175: The Commission concludes that the destruction of the Basma IVF clinic was a measure intended to prevent births among Palestinians in Gaza, which is a genocidal act under the Rome Statute and Genocide Convention. Para. 178: The Commission concludes that the ISF caused serious bodily and mental harm to members of this group, and deliberately inflicted conditions of life that were calculated to bring about the physical destruction of Palestinians in Gaza as a group, in whole or in part, which are categories of genocidal acts in the Rome Statute and the Genocide Convention. Para. 218: The Commission finds that the Israeli authorities have destroyed in part the reproductive capacity of the Palestinians in Gaza as a group, including by imposing measures intended to prevent births, one of the categories of genocidal acts in the Rome Statute and the Genocide Convention. Para. 219: The harm for pregnant, lactating and new mothers is of an unprecedented scale in Gaza…The underlying acts amount to crimes against humanity and deliberately inflicting conditions of life calculated to bring about the physical destruction of Palestinians as a group, one of the categories of genocidal acts in the Rome Statute and the Genocide Convention
     - ["UN Independent International Commission of Inquiry, legal analysis and recommendations on the implementation of the International Court of Justice Advisory Opinion, Legal Consequences arising from the Policies and Practices of Israel in the Occupied Palestinian Territory"](https://www.ohchr.org/sites/default/files/documents/hrbodies/hrcouncil/coiopt/2024-10-18-COI-position-paper_co-israel.pdf)(PDF). _UN Human Rights Council_. 18 October 2024. Retrieved 22 December 2025. Para. 23: On the issue of genocide, the Commission notes the provisional measures orders issued by the Court in the Application of the Convention on the Prevention and Punishment of the Crime of Genocide in the Gaza Strip (South Africa v. Israel). The Commission finds that all States are on notice that Israel may be or is committing internationally wrongful acts in both its conduct in the military operations in Gaza and its unlawful occupation of the West Bank, including East Jerusalem. Thus, the Commission finds that, unless States cease their aid and assistance to Israel in the commission of these acts, those States shall be deemed to be complicit in those internationally wrongful acts.

     - ["Application of the Genocide Convention (Order 24 V 24)"](https://www.icj-cij.org/sites/default/files/case-related/192/192-20240524-ord-01-00-en.pdf)(PDF). _International Court of Justice_. 24 May 2025. - Medzini, Meron. "Rabin and Hussein: From Enemies at War to Partners in Peace." in _The Palgrave Handbook of the Hashemite Kingdom of Jordan_ (Palgrave Macmillan, 2019) pp.435–446.
- [Anshel Pfeffer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anshel_Pfeffer "Anshel Pfeffer") (2018). _Bibi: The Turbulent Life and Times of Benjamin Netanyahu_, [Basic Books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Books "Basic Books"). [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_(identifier) "ISBN (identifier)") [978-0-465-09782-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-465-09782-1 "Special:BookSources/978-0-465-09782-1")
  - [Jonathan Freedland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Freedland "Jonathan Freedland"), "Trump's Chaver in Jerusalem" (review of [Anshel Pfeffer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anshel_Pfeffer "Anshel Pfeffer"), _Bibi: The Turbulent Life and Times of Benjamin Netanyahu_, Basic Books, 2018), _[New York Review of Books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Review_of_Books "New York Review of Books")_, vol. LXV, no 13 (16 August 2018), pp.32–34.
  - Adam Shatz, "The sea is the same sea" (review of [Anshel Pfeffer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anshel_Pfeffer "Anshel Pfeffer"), _Bibi: The Turbulent Life and Times of Benjamin Netanyahu_, Hurst, May 2018, [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_(identifier) "ISBN (identifier)") [978-1-84904-988-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-1-84904-988-7 "Special:BookSources/978-1-84904-988-7")), _[London Review of Books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Review_of_Books "London Review of Books")_, vol. 40, no. 16 (30 August 2018), pp.24, 26–28.
- [Remnick, David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Remnick "David Remnick"), "Hostages: As Benjamin Netanyahu clings to power, his country pays a price", _[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker "The New Yorker")_, 22 January 2024, pp.26–39. "\[Netanyahu's\] highest priority ... appeared \[to be\] to shake free of a series of criminal corruption indictments; he had been charged with everything from accepting illegal gifts ... to making a shady deal with a media baron to win favorable coverage. ... Netanyahu reclaimed the one position that provided refuge from prosecution ... At the end of 2022, he forged a hard-right coalition that allowed him to return as Prime Minister. He brought into the fold ... reactionaries \[who\] endorse the full annexation of the [West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank "West Bank") and have recently called for the expulsion of [Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip "Gaza Strip")'s \[Palestinian\] population. Netanyahu also pushed a wildly contentious 'judiical reform' law \[which\] perhaps more than half the country ... feared ... would undermine the Supreme Court, the [balance of powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balance_of_powers "Balance of powers"), and [democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy "Democracy") itself." (pp.32–33.)
